### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added and no score moved.** The cycle's output is a **specification-completeness audit** (all three priority confirmations written into the item 14 they govern, at v1.11, v1.9 and v1.9), the **completion of the transport pre-registration with the three numbers it named**, the **separation of the two transports** the record had conflated, the **correction of the inverse arm's length** (478 days, not 199), the **enumeration of the inverse panel's twelve near-empty dates** against the linear arm's zero, the **cascade day's complete 36-perpetual cross-section** and the eighteen delivery contracts that must be excluded from it, the discovery that the **funding archive is re-published vintage data** and the resulting **vintage rule**, the **84-asset common panel** that makes 004's metric sensitivity separable, and the adversarial re-test of all three carried disagreements with the counter-arguments recorded and declined.
- **One recorded figure is recorded as open rather than resolved**: the neighbouring-day ratio "41.24% (2024-08-07, **8 contracts**)" is not reproducible from the file census, which finds **32 perpetual contracts** (43 symbols including delivery contracts) with a file that day; the selection rule behind the 8-contract figure is not recorded.

## Previous cycle (forty-third research cycle)

A **scoring audit, specification and adversarial cycle.** All four totals re-derived from their seven dimension contributions and confirmed unchanged - **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58** (16+6+3+15+6+8+4), **CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46** (rejected; 12+3+3+15+3+6+4), **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 55** (12+3+6+15+3+8+8), **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 48** (12+6+3+12+3+8+4); rank order unchanged (001 > 003 > 004 by 3 and 7 points); all confidence low. Item 16 audited in all three places (contribution list, versioned narratives, unversioned rationale prose) with no stale scores remaining: 001 clean; 002 clean; 003's contribution list corrected to the seventeenth cycle's decomposition in the twenty-third cycle and re-checked; 004's rationale prose version-stamped as of v1.1 in the thirty-second cycle with the current state stated alongside. No hypothesis added; no score moved; no empirical validation performed; no return, spread, drawdown, hit rate, win rate, Sharpe ratio or strategy-performance quantity computed for any strategy.

**Key findings:**

1. **No score moved; all totals re-derived.** Every total re-derived from its seven dimension contributions and checked against the shortlist; no dimension moved. Item 16 audited in all three places - no stale score remains in any entry. The two *new* score-holding paragraphs appended by the thirty-fifth cycle (CRYPTO-EDGE-001's falsifiability alternative placement and CRYPTO-EDGE-003's falsifiability alternative placement) were found to carry an arithmetic error: each recorded the alternative as "total **52**" but a one-point move on falsifiability (weight 4) is 4 points, so the correct alternative placement is falsifiability 4/5 → 3/5, contribution 8 → 6, total **56** for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 (58 − 2) and total **53** for CRYPTO-EDGE-003 (55 − 2). Both corrections are marked superseded in place in IDEA_LEDGER.md; the alternative placements remain recorded rather than adopted.

2. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 - effective-sample bound as first-class validation-plan term; two-venue median resolved.** The entry's binding number is the independent forward-window count, not the trigger count: **180 at the 5-day horizon and 58 at the 20-day horizon** on the instantaneous construction, and only **36 independent 20-day windows** on the entry's re-registered slow clock (extended window raises these to 76 and 46-49). A registered null indistinguishable from an underpowered null is not publishable, so item 14 must carry the independent-window count at each registered horizon, the block structure a bootstrap must use (blocks sized to the dispersion level's one-to-three-hour half-life on the instant construction and the slow clock's weekly cadence), the minimum detectable effect implied by 36 slow-clock windows and 58 instantaneous windows, and a plain statement of whether the registered null is publishable without a pre-registered minimum detectable effect. On the two-venue median ambiguity: item 6 defines the trigger as venue-v rate minus the cross-venue median, which is not well defined on a two-venue set (the pair difference preserves every sign and trigger count but is twice the item-6 quantity in level terms). No third USDT-margined centralised venue with a free, dated, multi-year funding archive exists - OKX's public funding endpoint returns ~33 settlements and ignores `before`/`after`; Bybit's archive publishes no funding and no klines; Hyperliquid's free history is on a USDC-margined decentralised venue where the entry's persistence sub-claim is documented to fail (1.5-3.5 hour reversion against a 5-20 day horizon). Item 6 is restated as venue-v rate minus the *other venue's* rate (a pair difference, not a median), with the factor-of-two level difference declared and the trigger threshold rescaled accordingly.

3. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 - transport test pre-registered on the full inverse-arm sample; free stream capture status resolved.** The transport pre-registration, made in advance of any data inspection, declares over a pre-declared prospective window: (a) the cross-venue rank correlation of the daily forced-liquidation-share statistic between the entry's specified instrument and the second centralised venue's per-contract per-event record, (b) the disagreement rate at a pre-declared threshold on that statistic, and (c) the sign and magnitude of the mapping between the two venues' values, with the falsifier that a rank correlation below a pre-declared floor or a disagreement rate above a pre-declared ceiling retires the transport assumption. The 199-day inverse arm (2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14 on COIN-M) is the matched-sample leg: the same decomposition (de-duplicate on the full row, multiply by the per-symbol multiplier, read `side` as the order side) is run on matched days against the linear arm's p10-p90 band (p10 2.73%, p90 57.41%); the inverse arm's ratio on any matched day must fall inside that band to pass. **Forty-seventh-cycle correction, marked superseded in place: the inverse arm is a 478-calendar-day window extending 197 days beyond the linear arm's end, not a 199-day arm.** The free stream capture covers BTC/ETH from 2025-05-09 and XRP from 2024-11-09, but its burst density is not testable (the stream pushes only the latest order per symbol per 1000 ms, and the two products do not overlap in time), its licence and redistribution terms are unresolved, and it can substitute for the withdrawn USDⓈ-M product only on the post-2025 portions it covers - not on the entry's critical regime. The entry's critical regime remains unreachable on free data; no mirror of the withdrawn USDⓈ-M files has been located; the transport assumption remains untested and pre-registered rather than assumed.

4. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 - market-cap definition pre-registered; substituted screens' composition declared.** The free screen's small stratum is metric-determined: **447** assets below the reference ceiling ($60.3m bottom-quintile ceiling) under `CapMrktEstUSD` against **32** under `CapMrktCurUSD`, the two strata intersecting on **22** (Jaccard 0.048). A free replication must therefore state which market-cap definition it uses before any within-stratum comparison; `CapMrktEstUSD` (850 assets) is pre-registered as the definition and `CapMrktCurUSD` (135 assets) is declared as the robustness check. Because historical market capitalisation is not free, the substituted screens are listing-age (90-day minimum history) and trailing-volume (top 40 by trailing 30-day quote volume, reselected every 90 days, exit at top-60). The same metric-determination argument applies to the substituted screens: their own size composition - age distribution, trailing-volume distribution, overlap with the published market-cap screen's strata - must be declared alongside every within-stratum comparison, because listing age and trailing volume select on a different axis from market capitalisation and may select populations whose base momentum spread has a different sign (small coins reverse, large coins continue). The free screen's size composition is reported as a replication limitation; the reference screen's current-only membership remains a stated confound, benchmarkable only as of the check date.

5. **Adversarial and search.** All three carried disagreements re-tested and upheld (001 falsifiability 4/5; 003 free-data 5/5; 004 free-data 4/5), and the two alternative placements recorded by the thirty-fifth cycle are re-tested on their own terms: 001 falsifiability 4/5 vs 3/5 on the proxy's conditional unfidelity and 003 falsifiability 4/5 vs 3/5 on the 36-window effective sample - both remain recorded rather than adopted, with the standing-rule reason stated in each case. The select-versus-harvest prior was re-verified from the author's own published copy (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DavidAyusoPita/funding-carry-analysis/main/README.md`, HTTP 200, 8,981 bytes, plus the committed notebook at HTTP 200, 825,247 bytes and `src/analysis.py` at HTTP 200, 23,036 bytes); all three load-bearing statements stand verbatim (the negative control's 41-47% stability; the threshold rule at -9% to -15% annualised after costs; the patient harvest at +2.7% to +5.4% annualised net). No re-spin accepted; no hypothesis added; no score moved.

## Previous cycle (thirty-fifth research cycle)

A **construction, measurement and access-discovery cycle**. All four totals re-derived from their seven dimension contributions and confirmed unchanged - **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58** (16+6+3+15+6+8+4), **CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46** (rejected; 12+3+3+15+3+6+4), **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 55** (12+3+6+15+3+8+8), **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 48** (12+6+3+12+3+8+4); rank order unchanged (001 > 003 > 004, margins 3 and 7). All confidence low. Item 16 audited in all three places (contribution list, versioned narratives, unversioned rationale prose) with no stale scores remaining. The constructible pair for CRYPTO-EDGE-003 was **built** and its **episode count measured**; CRYPTO-EDGE-001's proxy residual decomposition was **measured as a distribution on the entry's own instrument**; a **mirror census outside the publisher's bucket** was run; CRYPTO-EDGE-004's **size composition was declared** against the published screen's; carried disagreements were re-tested and upheld and the select-versus-harvest prior re-verified from a reachable copy. No hypothesis was added and no score moved.

**Key findings:**
1. **No score moved; all totals re-derived.** Every total re-derived from its seven dimension contributions and checked against the shortlist; no dimension moved. Item 16 audited in all three places - no stale score remains in any entry. The catalogue's two previously-found prose defects (CRYPTO-EDGE-003's eleventh-cycle contribution list; CRYPTO-EDGE-004's v1.1 rationale prose) remain corrected in place and were re-checked.
2. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 - the constructible pair is built, and the episode count is measured.** The pair is assembled from the second venue's all-swap funding archive (1,738 daily files, 2022-01-17 to 2026-10-20, reproducing the recorded file count), its legacy per-instrument product (which supplies the 48 settlements the all-swap product lacks), and the publisher's monthly funding archive, and yields **5,201 matched settlements per instrument** (4,037 inside the entry's window) after one alignment rule: **both legs bucketed to the 8-hour funding grid**, because the second venue's ETH settlements carry a **+1 to +3 second** sub-minute offset on **310 of 5,213** rows while BTC's carry it on **2 of 5,213** - an exact-timestamp join would silently drop ~5.9% of ETH settlements and ~0.04% of BTC ones, asymmetrically. The legacy product's convention re-verified to the last digit on five overlapping days, **with one new finding: its first rate column is a *forecast* of the next period's rate, not a settled rate**, so the natural column choice measures the wrong quantity.
3. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 - the binding number is the independent-window count, not the trigger count.** On the entry's registered trigger the pair contains **741 trigger settlements (18.36%) and 310 episodes** for BTC-USDT-SWAP and **733 (18.16%) and 301** for ETH-USDT-SWAP over the entry's window - but de-clustering to non-overlapping forward windows gives **180 at 5 days and 58 at 20 days** (BTC; 180 and 56 for ETH), and on the entry's **re-registered slow clock** only **36** independent 20-day windows. **The entry's discriminative value is bounded by sample size, and the bound is now a number.** The dispersion is also transient at the trigger's own resolution: **85.8% and 88.5%** of extreme states are back inside the trailing 90-day band within **one** 8-hour interval. The negative control is re-run on the constructible pair with the prior art's own formula and lands at **53.2%/56.4%** sign stability - **outside the inherited 41-47% band**, which was measured on a different pair (Binance/Bybit) and a different grid, so the control must be re-specified on this pair rather than inherited.
4. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 - the proxy's residual decomposition is now a distribution, and the recorded 12.1% is pinned to the wrong numerator.** Measured on the entry's own specified instrument over its full 281-day record across the eight largest USD-M perps, the cross-sectional forced-SELL-to-open-interest-reduction ratio has **median 11.61%** (p10 2.73%, p90 57.41%) and the per-contract ratio **median 7.93%** over 1,120 contract-days (p10 1.47%, p90 47.90%, max 25.02). **The ratio is negatively related to the size of the reduction it must explain (corr -0.523): on the ten days with the largest open-interest reduction it is 1.54% to 13.81% (median 3.52%), against 26.2% on the smallest-reduction days.** The cascade-day figure reproduces exactly - **32,265,624 contracts across 36 contracts**, the recorded denominator - and the recorded **12.1%** is the *total* forced flow; the entry's registered **SELL-side** ratio is **11.20%**. The entry's registered statistic itself is now measured directly for the first time: forced SELL as a percentage of opening open interest, **median 0.176%**, p90 0.560%, max 1.728%.
5. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 - a free, dated forced-liquidation record for part of the post-July-2024 regime exists, but it is a different product.** The largest free third-party archive of the Binance dataset carries **no liquidationSnapshot group at all** in its own machine-readable coverage report, so **no free mirror of the withdrawn USD-M files exists**; but a free capture of the venue's public liquidation *stream* covers **BTC and ETH from 2025-05-09** and **XRP from 2024-11-09**, with `contains_gaps` declared per file. The entry's recorded obstacle is therefore **partially removed, not confirmed**: July to October 2024 is still uncovered, the capture is a throttled stream rather than a census, and its licence is unresolved.
6. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 - the free screen's size composition is metric-determined, not data-determined.** Under `CapMrktEstUSD` the free screen has **447** assets below the reference screen's $60.3m bottom-quintile ceiling, of which **405 are absent from the published screen**; under `CapMrktCurUSD` it has only **32**, of which 28 are absent. **The two free definitions' sub-ceiling strata intersect on 22 assets - a Jaccard of 0.048** - so the choice of market-cap definition changes the small stratum **fourteen-fold** and selects near-disjoint populations. The cross-check reproduces (median ratio **0.9982**, n = 288) with two genuine supply-definition outliers named (**xlm 2.96x, gno 3.78x**). Any free replication must **pre-register which market-cap definition it uses**.
7. **Adversarial.** All three carried disagreements re-tested and upheld (001 falsifiability 4/5; 003 free-data 5/5; 004 free-data 4/5), and two **alternative placements are recorded rather than adopted** (001 falsifiability 4/5 to 3/5 on the proxy's conditional unfidelity; 003 falsifiability 4/5 to 3/5 on the 36-window effective sample), each with the reason the standing rule forbids the move. The select-versus-harvest prior re-verified from the author's own published copy, which is reachable: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/DavidAyusoPita/funding-carry-analysis/main/README.md` (HTTP 200, 8,981 bytes) plus the committed notebook and `src/analysis.py`. **All three load-bearing statements stand verbatim**, and the notebook additionally pins the control's definition and exposes the prior art's "OKX publishes only three months of history" limitation as a **route artefact** of its REST-only data collection, which is what the archive route removes.
8. **One inherited instruction was found to be stale and was not executed as written.** The thirty-fifth task message's ground for CRYPTO-EDGE-003's liquidity-filter clause - "one-sided-and-prospective, second leg filtered from 2026-04-24 forward, first leg ends 2025-09-05, 232-day gap" - is the eighteenth and twenty-eighth cycles' and was **superseded in the thirty-second cycle**; the 2025-09-05 end was itself later corrected to 2025-09-07. The clause is finalised on the current ground: the filter is **two-sided and retrospective on 617 of the entry window's 1,346 dates and one-sided on 729**, extendable two-sided to 2023-12-31 to 2026-10-20 (813 days). A cycle that had formalised the inherited wording as a first-class clause would have re-introduced a superseded fact.

**Key findings:**
1. **No score moved; all totals re-derived.** Every total re-derived from its seven dimension contributions and checked against the shortlist; no dimension moved. Item 16 audited in all three places — no stale score remains in any entry.
2. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 — timestamp-corrected construction formalised.** The all-swap funding archive (`allswap-fundingrates`, settlement-instant timestamps) adopted as the canonical funding source; legacy per-instrument offsets corrected per instrument interval (instrument-dependent 1h/2h/4h/8h displacement). Join semantics documented: date-keyed joins both double-count and miss settlements because the two products partition the settlement stream differently. Corrected per-date coverage table recorded: the funding leg is continuous 2021-01-01 → 2026-10-20 with zero interior holes (2,119 days); the two-sided liquidity filter is executable one-sided on 729 dates and two-sided on 617 dates over 2022-01-01 → 2025-09-07, extendable to 813 two-sided days (2023-12-31 → 2026-10-20) if the window is extended to present. The sign remains untested — not guessed.
3. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 — denominator aligned to the 16:00 UTC bucket boundary.** Mean window-centre displacement 12.1h; the matching denominator row covers on average only 11.9 of 24 event hours. Residual scope gap quantified as a measured number, not an assertion: the three uncovered dated futures carry **0.33% of the BTC aggregate open interest ($10.96m of $3.297bn) and 0.22% of the ETH aggregate ($3.86m of $1.794bn)**. Live per-contract denominator measured at **786 contracts** (514 swaps, 272 dated futures); dated per-instrument history covers **782 of 786 (99.6%)**; 24h event densities reproduced with heavy clustering (busiest decile 35-67% of a day's events).
4. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 — composition-gradient control formalised.** Gradient measured directly (per-date stratum 60/100/138/47/208/144/150/388/491/449; all 2,850 dates populated; 95.8% of 5,323 exits upward price migrations, 4.2% panel deaths; forward 30-day graduation rate **6.1%**; daily upward exit rate **1.23%** of median stratum). For any within-stratum comparison the construction is **point-in-time re-cut per date** with assets whose series stop treated as **missing observations (not zero)**; the stablecoin exclusion uses the **stated list rule** (20 pegged tickers), adopted because the data-driven peg detector confounds pegs with illiquidity. The gradient's effect on within-stratum interpretation recorded as a **specification requirement**, not a performance estimate.
5. **Adversarial.** All three carried disagreements re-tested and upheld (001 falsifiability 4/5; 003 free-data 5/5; 004 free-data 4/5). The select-versus-harvest prior re-verified from the entry's own source (HTTP 200): the negative control (coin-flip sign, 41-47% stability), the threshold rule at **-9% to -15% annualised after costs** and the patient harvest at **+2.7% to +5.4% annualised net** all stand verbatim. Four candidate families examined, none cleared the bar. No re-spin accepted as a "data improvement" without a specification change and explicit falsifier.

## Previous cycle (thirty-third research cycle)

**Key findings:**
1. **The recorded 315-day funding hole does not exist; it was an artefact of probing one directory name.** The route `GET /api/v5/public/market-data-history?module=3&instType=SWAP&instFamilyList=ANY&dateAggrType=daily&begin={ms}&end={ms}` (keyless, access 1, at most ten days per call) returns, for the funding module, a second product: `…/traderecords/swaprates/daily/{YYYYMMDD}/allswap-fundingrates-{YYYY-MM-DD}.zip` — one CSV per day, schema `instrument_name, funding_rate, funding_time`, carrying every swap instrument's settlements. A HEAD sweep of every date from 2020-12-01 to 2026-10-21 (2,151 dates) finds the new product present on **2021-09-01 → 2021-09-30** and **2022-01-17 → 2026-10-20** (1,738 days), and the legacy per-instrument product present on **2021-01-01 → 2025-09-07** (1,711 days). **The union is one continuous interval, 2021-01-01 → 2026-10-20, with zero interior holes.** The venue's funding history therefore does not stop on 2025-09-07, and the pair **can be extended forward**.
2. **The funding leg is near-complete, not merely covered.** All 1,738 daily files were downloaded and the settlement streams rebuilt: BTC-USDT-SWAP, ETH-USDT-SWAP and BTC-USD-SWAP each carry **5,213 settlements against 5,214 expected 8-hour slots (99.98%)**, SOL-USDT-SWAP **5,204 (99.81%)**; the only interior holes are a venue-wide 8-hour outage on 2022-02-25 and an off-schedule settlement on 2022-12-18.
3. **The legacy funding archive's timestamp column is a live construction hazard, and the displacement is instrument-dependent.** The vendor's own header names the column 下一周期时间戳 — the *next period's* timestamp — while the new product and the REST route use the settlement instant. The two products carry identical rates displaced by exactly one funding interval: **8 hours** on BTC-USDT-SWAP (2025-09-07) and **4 hours** on the 4-hour instrument ARC-USDT-SWAP (same date). The venue runs 1h/2h/4h/8h intervals (8/131/121/8 instruments in the 2025-09-07 file), so the displacement is not a constant. The new product and the REST route agree exactly (6/6 settlements to 1e-13). The two products also partition the settlement stream differently, so the same settlement appears under two different *dates* in the two products: a date-keyed join both double-counts and misses settlements. **For CRYPTO-EDGE-003, whose registered signal is a cross-venue funding dispersion at matched timestamps, this is a specification clause, not a cosmetic issue.**
4. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003's symmetric liquidity filter is executable on a stated window, and the per-date coverage table is now recorded.** Over the entry's window 2022-01-01 → 2025-09-07 (1,346 days) the **publisher's leg is complete on every date** (BTCUSDT and ETHUSDT daily metrics present on all 1,346 dates, zero missing days each, established from the archive's key listings) and the **second venue's leg is present on 617 of 1,346 dates (45.8%)**, from 2023-12-31. The filter is one-sided on 729 dates and two-sided on 617; with the funding leg now continuous to 2026-10-20 and the second venue's per-instrument open interest gap-free to the same date, the two-sided window extends to **2023-12-31 → 2026-10-20 (813 days)** if the entry's window is extended to the present.
5. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 — the prospective arm's denominator is characterised to the contract, and its contemporaneity with the numerator is quantified.** The live per-contract denominator is **786 contracts** (514 swaps, 272 dated futures) plus an option book of 7,872 contracts of which **79.0% carry zero open interest**; the dated per-instrument history covers **782 of 786 (99.6%)**, the four exceptions being three newly listed dated futures (code 51001; together **0.33%** of the BTC aggregate open interest and **0.22%** of the ETH aggregate) and one test instrument — so the recorded statement that the scope error "falls to zero" is **qualified to a measured residual**. The route's 24-hour densities reproduce (ETH 2,047, BTC 1,131, SOL 499, PEPE 489, XRP 175, inverse 6) and its **clustering is quantified**: the busiest decile of hours holds 35-67% of a day's events and one hour can hold 44.9% of a family's. The **contemporaneity mismatch** between the 24-hour event window and the daily denominator is **0.0-24.0 hours of window-centre displacement (mean 12.1)**, with the matching denominator row covering on average only **11.9 of the 24 event hours**.
6. **The two open-interest products' daily rows are different quantities, and a naive reconciliation of them fails for that reason.** The aggregate's 1D row is *identical* to its 1H row at the same stamp (0.0000% on every date tested) — an end-of-window snapshot — while the per-contract 1D row matches neither the same-stamp hourly value nor the preceding 24-hour mean, and the vendor documents neither. A venue-level total built from the per-contract **daily** series therefore misses the aggregate 1D row by **median -1.61% (BTC), -1.29% (ETH), +0.96% (SOL)** with tails to **-9.95%/+11.41% (ETH)** and **+23.13% (SOL)**. With the semantics matched — per-contract **1H** values at the aggregate's stamp, summed — the reconciliation is **+0.09% to +1.76% (BTC), +0.22% to +0.88% (ETH), -0.02% to +0.94% (SOL)**: the two products are the same book, and the daily route's residual is a *semantic* difference, not a scope difference.
7. **The per-instrument daily open-interest product's breadth is measured across the whole venue.** A sweep of all 786 live instruments finds **782 carrying a daily series**; only **114** reach the product's floor of 2023-12-31, the rest starting at their own listing date (443 on the listing date, 198 one day before it because the daily row is stamped 16:00 UTC of the preceding day, 14 one day after). **Only 23 of the 782 have missing days inside their covered window** — 14 missing exactly one day at the listing boundary and 9 dated futures with multi-week gaps (up to 62 days) — and every series ends on 2026-10-20. The publisher's own daily metrics archive carries **1,038 of 1,039** USDⓈ-M symbols, with 18 stopping at 2025-03-10 and 14 at 2023-06-13 (expired dated contracts), so the publisher's leg has its own interior holes.
8. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 — the composition gradient is measured directly, the stablecoin rule is immaterial to it, and the panel can answer the reference screen's historical-membership question.** The per-date stratum reproduces to the digit (60 / 100 / 138 / **47** / 208 / 144 / 150 / 388 / 491 / **449**; all 2,850 dates populated); **95.8% of the 5,323 exits are upward price migrations and 4.2% panel deaths**; the **forward** 30-day graduation rate is **6.1%** and the daily upward exit rate **1.23% of the median stratum** — slow on any day, large over a month. The stablecoin rule is measured three ways (no rule 449 members / 93.2% retention; a **stated list of 20 pegged tickers** 445 / 93.1%; a **data-driven 90-day peg detector** 437 / 93.0%) and is **immaterial to the turnover measurement**; the detector's stratum members are mostly *fan tokens* whose prices are stale, so a data-driven peg rule confounds a peg with an illiquidity. Against today's $46.67m bottom-quintile threshold, **785 of 850** panel assets have ever exceeded it, **562 of 722** live assets have been in the sub-$61m stratum, and **525 of 722 have graduated from below $61m to at least $46.7m** — the screen's current membership is a survivorship-selected sample of a much larger population that has passed through its size band. The boundary cross-check re-run on a different matching rule: **376 matches, 287 comparable, median ratio 0.9986** (against the recorded 0.9997), panel final date still 2026-10-20.
9. **The vendor's file-index route re-tests the "no dated liquidation product" determination without path guessing, and strengthens it.** The route's modules are 1 (tick-by-tick trades), 2 (1-minute candlesticks), 3 (funding rate, swaps only), 4 (400-level L2 order book), 5 (5000-level L2 order book), 6 (50-level order book, options) and 11 (borrowing rate) — **no liquidation module exists**, and the route also discloses two L2 depth archives (the 400-level book for BTC-USDT-SWAP begins **2026-06-09**) and the all-swap / all-futures / all-spot tick and 1-minute candlestick archives.
10. **Adversarial:** all three carried disagreements re-tested and upheld (001 falsifiability 4/5; 003 free-data 5/5; 004 free-data 4/5). The **select-versus-harvest prior was re-verified** from the entry's own source (HTTP 200, 8,981 bytes): the negative control (coin-flip sign, 41-47% stability), the threshold rule at **-9% to -15% annualised after costs** and the patient harvest at **+2.7% to +5.4% annualised net** all stand verbatim. **Four candidate families were worked and none cleared the bar**: a cross-venue dispersion on the extended window (this is 003's own registered construction, recorded as a *feasibility result*); a settlement-aligned dispersion on each instrument's own funding interval (a *measurement specification* with no registered prediction, and the level is a market-wide state the prior covers); a dated depth-ladder spread on the second venue (four months deep, and a market-wide state); and a per-contract open-interest level-discontinuity signal on the daily cadence (the daily row's aggregation is undocumented and differs from the aggregate's by 1-2% at the median and up to 23% in the tail, so the measurement error is of the same order as the effects claimed). No re-spin accepted, no score moved, no empirical validation performed.

### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added and no score moved.** The cycle's output is a coverage correction that withdraws a recorded limit, two construction findings, three entries' coverage measured to the contract, and one catalogue-internal record-hygiene correction.
- **One recorded absence superseded by a later hit, and it was the catalogue's most consequential record.** The thirty-second cycle recorded a **315-day hole** in the venue's funding history and a clause that "the pair cannot be extended forward on venue-published funding data". Both are **superseded in place**: the vendor's own file-index route discloses a second funding archive under a different directory name, and the union of the two archives is **continuous from 2021-01-01 to 2026-10-20 with zero interior holes**. The catalogue's method of enumerating a vendor's documented *routes* was right; it was the method of enumerating a vendor's dated *files* that was missing.
- **Two recorded facts corrected in place.** (i) CRYPTO-EDGE-001's recorded statement that the prospective denominator's scope error "falls to zero" is **qualified**: three newly listed dated futures are outside the per-instrument product and carry **0.33% of the BTC aggregate open interest and 0.22% of the ETH aggregate**. (ii) The aggregate and per-contract open-interest products' **daily rows are not the same quantity**; the aggregate's is an end-of-window snapshot and the per-contract's is an undocumented aggregation, so the two must not be reconciled against each other on the daily cadence.
- **Two grounds added.** The funding archive's **timestamp convention** is now a declared construction requirement for CRYPTO-EDGE-003 (instrument-dependent one-interval displacement), and the **contemporaneity mismatch** between a 24-hour event window and a daily denominator is now a measured quantity for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 (0-24 h of displacement, mean 12.1 h).
- **One catalogue-internal record-hygiene correction.** CRYPTO-EDGE-003's eleventh-cycle narrative asserted "the current contributions are … (sum 52)" with no "state as of" stamp; the prose's tense is now audited, and the sentence corrected in place.
- **All five artefacts and both handoffs updated.** Research only; no performance statistics.

## Previous cycle (thirty-second research cycle)

An **access-discovery and record-correction cycle**. Enumerating the second centralised venue's own documentation surface surfaced a dated, per-instrument open-interest history that the catalogue had recorded as absent; the entry's recorded 232-day window gap is superseded in place as a result. Two further recorded facts were corrected (the prospective route's empty-page semantics; the funding archive's final day), a second class of stale-decomposition error was found in the catalogue's own score prose, and the free market-capitalisation panel's turnover measurement was extended from one stratum to five size bands and decomposed. No hypothesis was added and no score moved. Totals re-derived from seven dimension contributions: **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 55, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 48**. Rank order unchanged (001 > 003 > 004, margins 3 and 7). All confidence low.

**Key findings:**
1. **A dated, per-instrument open-interest history exists on the second venue, and it changes CRYPTO-EDGE-003's specification.** The route (`GET /api/v5/rubik/stat/contracts/open-interest-history?instId={INST}&period={PERIOD}[&end={ms}]`) was found by enumerating the venue's own documentation page (390 documented `/api/v5` paths) rather than by path-guessing. It is keyless, returns `[ts, oi contracts, oi base ccy, oi USD]`, serves 100 rows per page with `limit` ignored, and paginates backwards on `end` with zero page overlap. **Its depth is cadence-dependent**: daily reaches **2023-12-31** (1,025 daily rows, **100% coverage with zero missing days** over 2023-12-31 → 2025-09-07 for BTC-USDT-SWAP, ETH-USDT-SWAP, BTC-USD-SWAP and SOL-USDT-SWAP), while `1Dutc` reaches 2024-02-04, `4H` between 2026-01-15 and 2026-02-23, `2H` between 2026-06-23 and 2026-07-01, `1H` between 2026-08-15 and 2026-08-22 and `5m` between 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-18. The venue's documented claim that non-daily periods reach "early February 2024" **does not reproduce**.
2. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003's 232-day window gap is superseded in place, and the pair's symmetric liquidity filter becomes executable.** The funding archive's final daily file is **2025-09-07** (corrected from the recorded 2025-09-05, which had been located by probing two days past the true endpoint), so the venue's per-instrument open interest — which starts **2023-12-31** — now **overlaps** its funding archive over **617 days (45.8% of the entry's 1,347-day window)** instead of being disjoint by 232 days. The publisher's own per-contract daily open-interest archive covers the same dates, so **both legs** of the pair carry a per-contract series and the filter is constructible **two-sided and retrospectively**. The aggregate product's disjointness stands unchanged; it is the second product that changes the design. **The same work exposed the opposite constraint: the venue's funding history has a 315-day hole immediately after the entry's window.** The REST funding-history route retains exactly **278 settlements (92 days**, 2026-07-20 → 2026-10-21), the archive's last daily file is 2025-09-07, and the two pagination parameters behave **opposite to the vendor's documentation** (`after` goes back in time, `before` goes forward), so a validator following the documentation concludes from one page that only ~33 days exist. The venue therefore publishes **no funding history between 2025-09-08 and 2026-07-20**, and the pair **cannot be extended forward** on venue-published funding data; any extension must reconstruct funding from published premium-index history (an approximation to be declared) or stop at 2025-09-07.
3. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 — the prospective route's empty-page semantics are corrected in place, and the denominator's scope error is dissolved.** The recorded rule "an empty array appears only when the requested timestamp lies outside the retention window" is **falsified**: the inverse BTC-USD family returns empty arrays with code 0 for `after` = now − 22 h, − 20 h, − 18 h, − 16 h and − 15 h, all inside the window, because its six forced liquidations in 24 hours sit in a 1.05-hour burst 13.54–14.59 h before the request. **An empty page means "no events in the interval", so a walk's termination is not a retention-boundary detector for a thin family**; the boundary must be located by probing a ladder of ages, and re-located that way it lies between 23.5 h and 23.9 h. Everything else reproduced (exclusive cursor, zero duplicates over 4,352 events, `side`/`posSide` 4,352/4,352, contracts-unit `sz`, per-contract attribution), with a new observation that events are **clustered in time** rather than spread at a constant rate. The recorded **25.3% / 18.4% / 12.9%** scope errors were re-derived same-instant and reproduced (25.07 / 18.27 / 12.83) and are now **decomposed** into the coin-margined inverse swap (18.4 / 12.9 / 10.0% of the aggregate) and the dated-futures book (7.0 / 5.4 / 2.8%) — and because per-instrument history now exists, the error **falls to zero prospectively**: the denominator can be built on the same book and the same instruments as the numerator.
4. **The venue-wide "no dated liquidation product" determination is now backed by the vendor's own route catalogue.** Thirteen further REST paths, forty archive-template probes across ten directory names and the enumerated documentation page (**390 documented `/api/v5` paths, zero of them liquidation-related**) all confirm it; the venue's documentation describes its only liquidation feed as a websocket channel whose payload "doesn't represent the total number of liquidations on OKX". The regime gap for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 therefore remains **permanent and supply-side**.
5. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 — turnover is extended to five size bands and decomposed, and churn is shown not to be monotonic in size.** Median 30-day band-retention is 90.4% (<$10m), 88.7% ($10m–$61m), **83.8% ($61m–$250m)**, **83.1% ($250m–$1bn)** and 95.7% (>$1bn) — the **middle** bands turn over fastest, because an asset in the smallest band can only leave by growing. Survival is ~100% at the median in every band at 30 and 90 days, so the loss is **migration across a size boundary** (23.8 / 29.4 / 38.3 / 40.0 / 12.2% at the 180-day lag). Over the sub-$61m stratum's 2,824 daily transitions, **96.3% of the 5,292 exits are upward price migrations and 3.7% (194) are panel deaths** — so the stratum's non-stability is a *systematic composition gradient*, and the per-date cross-sectional construction is the only one consistent with it. The profile is **insensitive to the boundary choice** (30-day medians 94.3 / 95.3 / 96.6 / 98.0% for $46m / $61m / $100m / $250m boundaries) and the recorded 95.5% is reproduced at 95.3%. The **boundary cross-check re-run with stable-identifier matching** against a live reference screen gives **364 matches, 265 comparable, median ratio 0.9997** (264/265 within [0.5, 2]); the single outlier (`mpl` → *Syrup*, 0.016) is another identifier artefact, and the keyless reference tier's **`per_page` clamp at 100** is recorded because the screen must be paginated.
6. **A second class of stale-decomposition error was found in the catalogue's own score prose and corrected.** CRYPTO-EDGE-004's item 16 states its dimension scores in three places; the third — an *unversioned rationale prose* — had never been audited and still stated five superseded scores and a falsified confidence sentence. It is version-stamped and the current state is stated alongside it. The twenty-third cycle's rule (check the contribution list against the cycle record that moved the dimension) is extended to the prose.
7. **Adversarial:** all three carried disagreements re-tested and upheld (001 falsifiability 4/5; 003 free-data 5/5; 004 free-data 4/5); five candidate families were worked and **none cleared the bar** (a per-contract open-interest-change crowding state, an open-interest-share conditioning variable, a second-venue crowding-to-unwind test, a venue-level open-interest divergence trigger, and a per-contract open-interest filter for the prospective route — the last recorded as a construction improvement and the third as a specification addition to a deferral, not as hypotheses). The select-versus-harvest prior was **not** re-verified: its source returned HTTP 403 from this environment, so it stands exactly as recorded on 2026-10-21 and the cycle produced no evidence bearing on it. No re-spin accepted, no score moved, no empirical validation performed.

## Previous cycle (twenty-eighth research cycle)

A **measurement and specification cycle** in which the prospective forced-liquidation route was characterised end to end, the second venue's funding and open-interest windows were re-confirmed disjoint with the gap quantified, and the free market-capitalisation panel's small stratum was measured for membership turnover at full daily resolution. No hypothesis was added and no score moved. Totals re-derived from seven dimension contributions: **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 55, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 48**. Rank order unchanged (001 > 003 > 004, margins 3 and 7). All confidence low.

**Key findings:**
1. **The prospective route is now a complete specification, and the specification carries a quantified error term rather than an unknown.** The second centralised venue's per-event forced-liquidation route was re-walked live and pinned: retention **about 24 hours** with the cut-off located between 23.5 h and 23.9 h before the request; the `after` cursor is **exclusive** (a 4,565-event walk over five families produced **zero duplicate rows and zero page-boundary repeats**); units are **contracts**, with the multiplier published in the venue's own instrument specification (`ctVal` = 0.01 BTC for `BTC-USDT-SWAP`); semantics are pinned (`side` is the forced-flow direction and agrees with `posSide` on 3,360 of 3,360 events, and `time` equals `ts` on every event); per-contract attribution is available through the response group's `instId`. The free denominator is the venue's keyless per-underlying open-interest series, and its scope was **reconciled against per-instrument sums to within 0.06-0.33%**: it is every underlying's contracts across the swap **and dated-futures** books, excluding options. Against a linear-family numerator that overstates the denominator by **25.3% for BTC**, 18.4% for ETH and 12.9% for SOL, so a per-contract denominator on this venue requires **prospective collection**.
2. **The page is not a time depth, and the density varies twelve-fold across symbols on the same route.** 24-hour event counts ran ETH 2,152, BTC 1,202, PEPE 522, SOL 510, XRP 179 and inverse BTC-USD 6; the first page covered 4.09 h (ETH) to 12.24 h (XRP) and a full walk needed 2 to 22 pages. A fixed page budget is therefore not a fixed lookback, and the page-to-time mapping must be measured per symbol. The de-duplication rule must be keyed on the **full row**, not the millisecond: distinct events share a millisecond on 1.8% of BTC rows and 1.7% of ETH rows.
3. **The second venue publishes no dated liquidation product of any kind, so the prospective-only classification is now a venue-wide determination rather than a property of one route.** Six REST paths (including `liquidation-volume`, `liquidation-orders-history` and `liquidation-orders-archive`) and six archive templates (`openinterest`, `open-interest`, `oi`, `liquidation`, `liquidations`, `forced`) all return HTTP 404. The regime gap for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 therefore remains **permanent and supply-side**, and the prospective arm remains the only live forced-liquidation route on any venue.
4. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003: the two windows are re-confirmed disjoint and the gap is quantified; one recorded error behaviour is superseded in place.** The funding archive was re-verified (monthly 2021-01 to 2025-09, daily per instrument to 2025-09-05, candles 2022-01-01 to present) and the open-interest route was re-measured at all three cadences it supports (180 daily rows from 2026-04-24; 720 hourly rows from 2026-09-21; 576 five-minute rows from 2026-10-19). The windows are therefore **disjoint by 232 days**, the symmetric liquidity filter is executable on **no date**, and the filter remains **one-sided and prospective**. Correction: the recorded "five-minute cadence errors at 30 days or more" did not reproduce — a 40-day window returned code 0 with the same 48-hour payload, so the reproducible behaviour is **silent truncation**.
5. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004: the small stratum's turnover is measured for the first time, and the recorded "90x boundary divergence" is withdrawn as a matching artefact.** The full panel was pulled at daily resolution (118 pages, 1,172,283 rows, zero errors). The sub-$61m stratum is populated at every date but is neither monotone nor stable (60 in mid-2019, 138 in early 2021, **47 in January 2022**, 208 in 2023, 491 in August 2026, 449 now), loses a **median of one member per day** (67% of days lose at least one), and retains only **95.5% of its members after 30 days** at the median, 79.4% at the 10th percentile and 44.3% in the worst month. The churn is *not* panel turnover (the panel's own 30-day overlap is 100% at the median); it is price movement across the boundary plus data stopping. The current-only membership constraint is quantified: **128 of 850 assets (15.06%) have series ending before 2026-10-20 and 123 of them entered the small stratum at some point**. And for 277 correctly ticker-matched names the panel-to-reference market-cap ratio has a **median of 0.998** (small stratum: 0.990) — every extreme outlier in the earlier record is a **mixture of ticker collisions and same-name supply-definition differences**, not a population property of the panel — the panel's `u` is *Union* against the reference's *United Stables* (0.000x), and its `gno` is $1,145.6m against the reference's *Gnosis* $303.0m (3.8x) — while the 277 correctly matched names agree to a median of 0.998.
6. **Adversarial:** all three carried disagreements re-tested and upheld (001 falsifiability 4/5; 003 free-data 5/5; 004 free-data 4/5); the select-versus-harvest prior was re-verified against the entry's own source, re-fetched live on 2026-10-21 — the negative control (CEX-versus-CEX spread centred on zero, sign a coin flip, 41-47% stability), the threshold rule at **-9% to -15% annualised after costs** and the patient harvest at **+2.7% to +5.4% annualised net** all stand verbatim. No re-spin accepted, no score moved, no empirical validation performed.

### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added and no score moved.** The cycle's output is measurement and specification: a fully characterised prospective transport route with a quantified denominator scope error (CRYPTO-EDGE-001), a re-confirmed and quantified 232-day window gap on the pair's liquidity filter (CRYPTO-EDGE-003), and a first measurement of the small stratum's membership turnover with a corrected boundary cross-check (CRYPTO-EDGE-004).
- **Two recorded facts corrected in place.** (i) The second venue's five-minute open-interest route does **not** error at 30-day windows; it **silently truncates to the most recent 48 hours**, and the reproducible statement replaces the recorded error. (ii) The recorded "market-cap divergences up to about 90x at the stratum boundary" is **withdrawn as a data statement** and recorded as a symbol-matching artefact; for correctly matched names the two panels agree to within about 1% (median ratio 0.998 over 277 names).
- **Two recorded grounds sharpened with dated replacements.** The prospective route's limits are now stated as *measured* facts (retention between 23.5 h and 23.9 h; page-to-time mapping per symbol; no dated liquidation product anywhere on the venue), and the free screen's small stratum's ground is restated as a *turnover* profile with the current-only membership cost quantified (128 of 850 assets; 123 of them former stratum members).
- **All five artefacts and both handoffs updated.** Research only; no performance statistics.

## Previous cycle (twenty-third research cycle)

A **measurement and record-correction cycle**: three recorded facts were tested against live data and three were falsified, one of them the catalogue's own. No hypothesis was added and no score moved. Totals re-derived from seven dimension contributions: **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 55, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 48**. Rank order unchanged (001 > 003 > 004, margins 3 and 7).

**Key findings:**
1. **One correction to the catalogue's own arithmetic, and a new class of error for the catalogue.** CRYPTO-EDGE-003's score line carried the **seventh** cycle's contribution list (evidence quality 2/5 -> 6, free-data accessibility 4/5 -> 12) while stating the total the **seventeenth** cycle had reached, so the line summed to 55 **by accident** — the seventh cycle's evidence-quality downgrade (2/5 -> 1/5, -3) and the seventeenth cycle's free-data upgrade (4/5 -> 5/5, +3) had cancelled in the total while swapping which dimension carried the move. Corrected in place to the seventeenth cycle's decomposition, **12+3+6+15+3+8+8 = 55**. The total was right and the decomposition was wrong, which a total-against-shortlist check cannot detect.
2. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001: the prospective forced-liquidation route works better than recorded.** The second centralised venue's `liquidation-orders` endpoint **paginates** on its `after` parameter and retains **about 24 hours** (a 15-page walk returned 1,500 events with no gaps, reaching 2026-10-20 00:08:16 UTC), against the seventeenth cycle's recorded "about five hours" and "no working pagination"; the empty array appears only outside the retention window. The publisher-side determination is re-verified unchanged (three bucket prefixes; the relocated USDⓈ-M record live at `data3/liquidationSnapshot/`; the documented UM path enumerating zero objects), so the regime gap remains permanent and supply-side.
3. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003: the recorded ground on the second leg's liquidity filter is superseded in place.** A dated daily open-interest product *does* exist on the second venue (keyless, 180 daily rows, 2026-04-24 to 2026-10-20, `begin`/`end` honoured, longer windows silently truncated, 5-minute cadence erroring at 30 days) — but the second venue's funding archive (2021-01 to 2025-09-05) and its open-interest window are **disjoint**, so the pair's symmetric liquidity filter is executable on **no date**, and the filter must be declared one-sided and prospective.
4. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004: the free market-cap panel is about seven times deeper than recorded, and the recorded boundary example is falsified.** The community tier carries a market-cap metric on **901** assets, not 135 (135 current, 850 estimated), and the estimated panel populates the small stratum throughout the window (61 assets below $61m in mid-2019, 450 today, against 14 to 32 on the current metric). The recorded "up to about 90x at the boundary (VET $6.74m vs $614.0m)" is wrong on measurement: the panel's VET is $579.23m against the reference's $616.67m (ratio 0.94), and the $6.74m figure is OMG's ($6.81m). Corrected cross-check: median panel-to-reference ratio **0.97** for matched names, so the surviving ground is membership, and the new ground is that the reference screen's membership is current-only.
5. **A scope conflict in the inherited priorities, recorded and re-specified.** "Measure the unconditional spread", "within-stratum comparison with a size-matched control" and "test thresholded vs unthresholded patient carry" are **return statistics** and are forbidden in this repository; they have been carried as cycle tasks for four cycles. They are re-specified as validation actions for elsewhere, and replaced by the research-level tasks this cycle executed.
6. **Adversarial:** the carried falsifiability disagreement on 001 (4/5 -> 5/5, total 60) re-tested and declined again on a ground the cycle strengthened; all other carried grounds hold. No hypothesis added, no score moved, no empirical validation performed.

**New this cycle:** No hypothesis added. No score moves. Three recorded facts corrected in place (one of them the catalogue's own arithmetic, one a data-route record, one a boundary-divergence example), two recorded grounds superseded in place with dated replacements, and one scope conflict in the inherited priorities recorded and re-specified. All artefacts updated.

## Previous cycle (fifteenth research cycle)

A **closure-and-correction cycle with one decisive data find**. No hypothesis was added and no score moved. What the cycle produced is **the location of the withdrawn USDⓈ-M liquidation record — it was relocated, not destroyed, and it is live, free and checksum-verifiable at an undocumented path in the publisher's own bucket** — **five corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts**, **the first measurement of a cascade's per-event path (a negative result)**, **the decomposition of the open-interest proxy the catalogue has used since the eleventh cycle**, **the first free, dated, point-in-time market-capitalisation panel found for the universe at all**, and **the answers to four of the five external questions the cycle inherited**.

1. **The withdrawn USDⓈ-M record was relocated, not destroyed, and it is live, free, dated and checksum-verifiable at an undocumented path: `data3/liquidationSnapshot/`.** Verified 2026-10-20 by direct listing of the publisher's own bucket (access 1, keyless): **311 symbol directories, 64,820 daily zips plus 64,820 `.CHECKSUM` sidecars (129,640 objects)**, coverage **2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 (281 days)** — exactly the range the publisher's own historical-data FAQ documents for the "Liquidation Snapshot" product — with `BTCUSDT`, `ETHUSDT`, `SOLUSDT`, `DOGEUSD`… `LINKUSDT` and `ADAUSDT` present for all 281 days. The documented path `data/futures/um/daily/liquidationSnapshot/` now lists **zero** objects and its objects return HTTP 404. Three files were verified end to end, each matching its published SHA-256 sidecar byte for byte: `BTCUSDT-liquidationSnapshot-2023-08-01.zip` (13,928 bytes, 1,386 data rows = exactly 2 x 693 unique rows), `ETHUSDT-…-2024-03-31.zip` (12,346 bytes, 1,158 rows = 2 x 579) and `BTCUSDT-…-2024-03-31.zip` (15,296 bytes, 1,544 rows = 2 x 772). Every object carries `LastModified` 2025-04-16, so this is a **single bulk relocation**, not a series of revisions, and it explains why the publisher's own issue thread reported the old path as "entirely removed". **Consequence: CRYPTO-EDGE-001's construction question is closed. The entry's registered statistic — sell liquidations as a percentage of opening open interest — is now directly measurable, at per-event resolution, on the entry's own specified instrument.** The record's *window* still ends 2024-03-31 (2024-10-14 on the inverse family), so the entry's critical post-July-2024 regime is still not covered, and the entry's registered prediction still cannot be tested where it matters most; the obstacle has changed shape, not disappeared.

2. **The USDⓈ-M family has no contract multiplier, and the statistic is natively in USDT.** The USDⓈ-M `original_quantity` is in the **base asset**, not in contracts: `BTCUSDT-liquidationSnapshot-2023-08-01` contains a SELL of `0.036` BTC at `29,105.23`, and the publisher's own USDⓈ-M `metrics` file for the same day gives `sum_open_interest` 108,876.86 BTC against `sum_open_interest_value` 3,181,469,038 USDT, an implied price of 29,220.80 consistent with the day's kline. Notional is `quantity x price`; the open-interest denominator is `sum_open_interest_value`. The multiplier rule belongs to the **inverse** family only.

3. **The multiplier census is now complete, and the answer is binary.** Deriving `multiplier = sum_open_interest_value x close_price / sum_open_interest` for **all 118 symbols** in the COIN-M tree, using two independent venue files per symbol: **109 symbols are 10 USD per contract and 9 are 100 USD** (the 8 BTC dated quarters and BTCUSD_PERP), with the value computed at the close running 9.924-10.020 and 99.932-100.097 respectively. There is **no $1, $5 or other multiplier anywhere in the tree**, and the 73 delivery contracts behave exactly like the 45 perpetuals. (Values computed at the day's open are noisier, up to 2.35 units off, because the opening snapshot, the opening price and the first five-minute mark are not simultaneous; the close-based value is the one to use.)

4. **The catalogue's "49 re-uploaded files" figure is wrong by a factor of fifteen, and the revision structure is different.** A complete `LastModified` census of the 17,182-file liquidation tree gives **16,445 files at the expected T+1 lag and 737 that deviate**, spread across **21 campaigns on 17 distinct dates** — 664 files at a two-day lag concentrated in a continuous re-publication campaign between 2023-10-29 and 2023-11-21, 24 files at a three-day lag on 2024-06-25, and 49 files carrying the single date 2026-04-16 that the catalogue recorded (covering 2023-09-25, 2023-11-20, 2024-04-18, 2024-06-11 and 2024-06-12). The fourteenth cycle's own two numbers were mutually inconsistent — 16,445 at one-day lag plus 49 deviating cannot both be true of 17,182 files — and 737 is the reconciling value. **Consequence: the archive's revision history is broader than the catalogue recorded, the 2026-04-16 remediation is one campaign among twenty-one rather than the only one, and the "one-day lag" baseline is itself an artefact of the majority of files rather than a rule.**

5. **The cross-sectional denominator was wrong, and the cascade-day cross-section was complete rather than 80% covered.** The fourteenth cycle used "45 COIN-M perps" as the cross-sectional denominator. Reconstructing the live universe from the publisher's own `metrics` tree, on 2024-08-05 the live universe was **36 contracts** — and all 36 have a liquidation file. The nine that do not are **not missing data**: five (`APEUSD_PERP`, `ICXUSD_PERP`, `KNCUSD_PERP`, `ROSEUSD_PERP`, `XTZUSD_PERP`) were **delisted** before the date, with their `metrics` series ending 2024-03-03 and their klines carrying **zero volume and a frozen close**; `XMRUSD_PERP` was delisted 2024-02-15; and three (`DOGSUSD_PERP`, `SUIUSD_PERP`, `WLDUSD_PERP`) were **not yet listed** (klines begin 2024-09-10, 2024-09-18 and 2024-10-02). **Consequence: the cross-section on the cascade day is 36 of 36 live contracts — 100% coverage — and the registered statistic's full range is 0.26% (TRXUSD_PERP) to 37.94% (ENSUSD_PERP) with a median of 1.87%, a total of 3,612,966 SELL contracts ($65.2m at the per-symbol multipliers), and an open-interest reduction negative for all 36 contracts.** The re-derivation reproduces every published figure of the fourteenth cycle to the digit except the median proxy-to-flow ratio, which is **9.44**, not the recorded 9.94.

6. **The "a symbol-level absence may be a zero-event day" reading is not supported, and the publisher's own open-interest series settles it.** Consulting the `metrics` tree for the dates the catalogue called ambiguous: on **2023-09-09**, 41 of 45 perps are live and 39 of them have **no** liquidation file, yet **10 of those 39 had open interest fall by more than 1% that day** (ENSUSD_PERP -4.94%, BCHUSD_PERP -4.22%, RUNEUSD_PERP -3.58%), and the day before (2023-09-08) shows 19 live-but-absent contracts of which 6 fell more than 1% (ICXUSD_PERP -11.89%). The same pattern holds on 2023-09-22 (9 of 34), 2023-09-23 (12 of 35), 2023-10-14 (6 of 36), 2024-06-11 (7 of 31) and 2024-06-12 (17 of 32). On 2023-09-25 and 2024-04-17/18 the **`metrics` tree is missing too**, so those dates are venue-wide failures and not liquidation-specific ones — a correction to the fourteenth cycle's classification. **Consequence: an absent liquidation file must be treated as a missing observation, never as a zero, unless the contract's own open-interest series is demonstrably unchanged; the zero-event reading is the exception, not the default.**

7. **The per-event path carries no information beyond the daily aggregate — a clean negative result on the cascade-ordering mechanism.** On 2024-08-05 the first forced liquidation in all 36 live contracts falls inside a **55.3-minute window** (00:02:30 to 00:57:49 UTC) and **50% of the day's forced SELL contracts are recorded in the first 57 minutes**; on the adjacent days the same measure is **910.3 minutes** (2024-08-04) and **1,242.3 minutes** (2024-08-06), so the cascade day is 16x and 22x more compressed. The rank order of first liquidation is *negatively* correlated with contract size on all three days (Spearman -0.50, -0.58, -0.59 against opening open interest; -0.42, -0.73, -0.59 against the day's forced SELL contracts), which is the signature of an exponential-arrival process in which the first event is a function of the event rate. **Consequence: the "path" is a liquidity ordering, not a propagation order, and a hypothesis that reads cascade sequencing off this record would be reading the cross-section's size ordering.** This is the cycle's answer to the map-in-path question, and it is negative.

8. **The open-interest proxy has been decomposed, and the decomposition is adverse: the proxy is mostly measuring voluntary de-risking.** On 2024-08-05 the recorded forced liquidations across the 36 live contracts (3,612,966 SELL and 288,128 BUY contracts) explain only **12.1%** of the 32,265,624-contract cross-sectional open-interest reduction; the median contract's forced liquidations are 11.11% of its own net open-interest reduction (min 3.09% for DOTUSD_PERP, max 177.83%). The same figure is 24.8% on 2024-08-04 and 4.9% on 2024-08-06. The residual is either voluntary closing or liquidations the file does not capture, and **both readings are adverse** to the use of the open-interest-clearing proxy as a stand-in for "the forced unwinding", which is what the catalogue has used since the eleventh cycle. The proxy-to-flow ratio of 6.9x-9.9x that the catalogue published is the same fact seen from the other side.

9. **The event count for any forced-unwind hypothesis is now quantified.** Over a random 24-day sample of the record plus a contiguous 62-day window (2024-07-01 to 2024-08-31), the cross-sectional median of the registered statistic is **0.0052% of opening open interest on an ordinary day**, and the distribution is extremely heavy-tailed: **7 of 75 observed days reach a 0.05% median**, **2 reach 0.5%**, and the cascade day sits at **1.87%** with a maximum contract at 37.94%. The 2024-07-05 day is the only other extreme in the window (median 0.70%, 15 of 26 contracts above 0.5% of open interest). **Consequence: a hypothesis whose trigger is an extreme cross-sectional forced-unwind state has at most a handful of usable events in the entire 478-day record — roughly 5-8 at a loose threshold, 1-2 at the extreme — and the event count, not the statistic, is the binding constraint.** This is the first time the catalogue has been able to state the event count for this family rather than infer it.

10. **A free, dated, point-in-time market-capitalisation panel exists — the first one found for the universe at all.** The Coin Metrics v4 **community** API (`https://community-api.coinmetrics.io/v4/timeseries/asset-metrics?assets=btc&metrics=CapMrktCurUSD`, access 1, keyless, HTTP 200) returns a daily `CapMrktCurUSD` series with history to genesis for the majors (BTC 2010-08-24, ETH 2015-11-06, LTC 2013-05-20, DOGE 2014-03-16, XRP 2014-10-02, ADA 2018-01-14, BNB 2017-08-30) and the asset catalogue shows **135 assets with a community-tier market-cap metric** out of 6,347. Every route the catalogue previously recorded as checked and empty is confirmed empty: CoinGecko (HTTP 429 on historical queries), CoinPaprika (402), CoinLore (403), CoinMarketCap (500), DefiLlama (404 on all five per-token supply forms), Blockchair (430/402), Etherscan v2 (key required), Dune (401), Santiment (404), Messari (404), Kaiko (403), Blockscout and mempool.space (current only), Signals Analytics (404). **Consequence: the catalogue's recorded determination that "historical market capitalisation and market-cap rank remain unavailable on every free tier" is superseded in place.** The limitation is now coverage rather than access: the free panel is 135 large and mid-cap assets, survivor-only and listing-biased, so it cannot reach the smallest size quintile in which the published base effect is documented, and a turnover proxy from the venue's own klines is a **liquidity** proxy confounded with the very effect the stratum must control for.

11. **The venue-pair question for CRYPTO-EDGE-003 is answered, and the answer is a revision rather than a substitution.** The entry's own source records that the CEX-versus-DEX spread's *level* mean-reverts with an AR(1) half-life of 1.5-3.5 hours while its *direction* is a regime lasting months worth 8.5-11.4% annualised, and that "the same measurement between two centralised venues is centred on zero". A 5-20 day horizon is 40-320 half-lives away from the free pair's level, and the centralised pair's zero-centred spread makes an "extreme" reading a rare, near coin-flip event. Independent multi-venue work is consistent: the mean CEX-versus-DEX funding difference is reported as negligible (0.18 basis points) with CEX-versus-CEX correlations above 0.4 against 0.175 for CEX-versus-DEX, and a separate significance-tested study of a DEX against two centralised venues reports a structurally persistent differential of roughly 7% annualised with no measured reversion horizon — a carry, not a dispersion. **Consequence: the claim must be narrowed to two centralised venues or reframed as carry, and the free second venue is the pair on which the entry's own source documents the thresholded rule finishing at -9% to -15% annualised after costs.** The fourteenth cycle's recorded free-data upgrade to 5/5 is therefore **answered against the upgrade**, and a new, opposing recorded disagreement (4/5 -> 3/5, total 49) is recorded and declined.

12. **No hypothesis was added and no score moved: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45**, each re-derived from its seven dimension contributions (score / 5 x weight, summing to 100) and checked against the table below before publication. The standing rule holds: evidence bearing on measurement, cost, data access or a base effect does not move a score. **Five recorded disagreements are now carried**: falsifiability 4/5 -> 5/5 for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 (total 60, now with one of its two grounds removed), the **answered** free-data 4/5 -> 5/5 for CRYPTO-EDGE-003 (total 55) and a new, opposing 4/5 -> 3/5 (total 49) for the same entry, the **answered** free-data 3/5 -> 2/5 for CRYPTO-EDGE-004 (total 43) and a new, opposing 3/5 -> 4/5 (total 48) for the same entry. The one family the cycle's data could have generated — forced-unwind intensity as a cross-sectional *ranking* variable — is recorded as a **rejected re-spin**, with the reasons.
