### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added.** The cycle's new family candidate — forced-unwind intensity as a cross-sectional *ranking* variable — was tested against the catalogue's own rejected families and **rejected as a re-spin**, with the reasons recorded in REJECTED_AND_DEFERRED.md.
- **One decisive access finding and four corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts.** The relocated USDⓈ-M record at `data3/liquidationSnapshot/` (311 symbols, 64,820 files, 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31, SHA-256-verifiable, access 1) closes CRYPTO-EDGE-001's construction question; the "49 re-uploaded files" figure is superseded by **737 across 21 campaigns**; the "45 perps" cross-sectional denominator is superseded by a live universe of **36** (cross-section complete); the "a symbol-level absence may be a zero-event day" reading is superseded by the publisher's own open-interest series; and the multiplier census is completed at **10 USD / 100 USD with no other value**.
- **Two new process rules and one new standing constraint** recorded in PROCESS_CHANGELOG.md: *a documented removal is not a deletion, and a bucket must be censused for undocumented sibling prefixes before a record is written off*; *a cross-sectional denominator is the live universe, and the live universe is an observable, not a file count*; *an event ordering is not a path if it is a rate ordering*; and *a proxy's residual must be decomposed before the proxy is used as a stand-in*.
- **Four of the five inherited external questions answered**, and the fifth (a free per-contract forced-liquidation record for the October 2025 cascade) answered negatively with a dated reason: the Zenodo panel is daily and per-asset rather than per-contract, the free aggregator tiers are current-only or rolling, the publisher's own archive ends 2024-10-14, and the only free per-contract candidate is a decentralised venue's millisecond record whose free download route is described but not independently confirmed.

## Previous cycle (fourteenth research cycle)

A **correction-and-closing cycle**. No hypothesis was added and no score moved. What the cycle produced is **four corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts** — three of them silent-failure defects in the record the thirteenth cycle discovered — **three primary venue documents that close the open question about the record's end and reverse part of the thirteenth cycle's headline access finding**, **the first cross-sectional measurement of forced unwinds on that record**, **a determinate answer, in both directions, to the second-venue funding question the twelfth and thirteenth cycles carried as open**, and **the verification and three-fold corroboration of the size-momentum lead** that was recorded as a lead and not as evidence. Three of the four corrections are to numbers the catalogue itself published.

1. **The record's end is now explained by a dated primary venue document, and the archive's *history* is not what the catalogue recorded.** In the publisher's own data repository, a member's report that the USDⓈ-M `liquidationSnapshot` files were missing was answered by a repository collaborator on **2024-10-15** with "**this data is no longer provided**", and closed the same day — **24 hours after the last file was written**, at the normal one-day publication lag (16,445 of the tree's 17,182 files have a one-day lag, and the last one has a one-day lag). A second collaborator statement dated **2026-10-06** says it again and adds the two facts the catalogue needed: "liquidationSnapshot is no longer provided on data.binance.vision. **USD-M data has been removed**, and the **last COIN-M file is 2024-10-14**." A third primary document is the venue's own historical-data FAQ, which still lists a "Liquidation Snapshot" product with the range **2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31**, and a repository issue is titled "USDT-M liquidationSnapshot data missing **after 2024-03-31**". **Consequence: the USDⓈ-M liquidation record that the catalogue recorded as never having existed did exist, for 281 days, on CRYPTO-EDGE-001's own specified instrument, and has since been removed from the archive.** The thirteenth cycle's supersession is extended rather than repeated: "no liquidation dataset in the UM tree" is true of the archive *today* and false of the archive's *history*, and the entry's remaining obstacle is now a **retrieval** question, not a construction question. A fourth detail from the same source is a gift to the audit trail: the exact-two row duplication was **publicly visible in the publisher's own repository from 14 June 2024** ("why is each row double?") and still has no staff explanation, two years before this catalogue found it independently.

2. **The record's file set is not a daily panel, and the absence is structured — a fourth silent-failure defect.** Over the whole tree the 118 symbols' own spans total **26,834 symbol-days** against **17,182 published files**, so **9,652 symbol-days (36.0%) are absent**, and **110 of the 118 symbols have internal gaps**; no symbol is contiguous. The absence is strongly non-random in symbol (**1.3%** for BTCUSD_PERP, **0.8%** for ETHUSD_PERP, against **47.6%** for TRXUSD_PERP and **62.2%** for ICXUSD_PERP) and strongly co-missing in date: on **219 dates at least 10 of the live perps are simultaneously absent**, the worst being **2023-09-09 and 2023-09-25 at 39 of the 41 live perps (95.1%)**. Two of those are *venue-wide* failures visible in the other trees — **2024-04-18** (also missing from the CM klines and trades trees) and **2024-06-11 and 2024-06-12** (also missing from the CM klines, trades and metrics trees) — while 2023-09-09 and 2023-09-23 are missing only from the liquidation tree, so the failure is product-specific on some dates. The publisher's own remediation is visible in the bucket: **49 files were re-uploaded on 2026-04-16** for exactly the five previously-absent dates 2023-09-25 (3 files), 2023-11-20 (6), **2024-04-18 (33)**, 2024-06-11 (4) and 2024-06-12 (3) — a complete remediation of the venue-wide date and a partial one of the market-wide date. **The two readings of an absent file cannot be separated from the archive alone, and the catalogue does not choose between them**: symbol-level absence is coherent with a zero-event day (sampled event counts on present days run from 1-2 for AAVEUSD_PERP to 57-154 for BTCUSD_PERP; absent days are systematically quieter, pooled median absolute daily return 0.96% against 1.79%, and over-represented at weekends), while date-level absence is not (39 of 41 perps absent on one date is not a market with no forced liquidation anywhere). A second venue's archive documents the same convention — Hydromancer states that files "are only created when non-empty" — which supports the symbol-level reading as a *convention* rather than an assumption. **Mandatory consequence: an absent file must never be read as a zero-event day without a declared treatment, and any cross-sectional breadth statistic must have its denominator fixed to the live universe rather than to the files present.**

3. **The contract multiplier is per symbol, and the recorded "$100" rule is corrected in place.** The multiplier was derived for eleven symbols by pairing two independent free venue files — the metrics file's `sum_open_interest` (contracts) and `sum_open_interest_value` (the coin) against the same day's daily kline open — as `multiplier = value x open_price / open_interest`: **100.02 for BTCUSD_PERP and 99.997 for BTCUSD_240628, and 10.000 for ETHUSD_PERP, 9.999 for ETHUSD_241227, 10.003 for XRPUSD_PERP, 9.993 for DOGEUSD_PERP, 10.005 for BNBUSD_250328, 9.961 for SOLUSD_250328, 10.00 for LINKUSD_PERP**. Consequence: the naive `quantity x price` product overstates a contract's forced notional by the coin's own USD price — **528x for BTCUSD_PERP** on 2024-08-05, as the thirteenth cycle computed, but roughly **2,400x for ETHUSD_PERP** — so the thirteenth cycle's headline figure is a **BTC-specific** number and the general error is *larger*, not smaller.

4. **The "10-14x" proxy-to-flow ratio the catalogue published is a mis-statement of its own two percentages, and the ratio is not stable in either dimension.** The flow is 14.4% (BTCUSD_PERP) and 10.1% (ETHUSD_PERP) of the open-interest reduction, so the reduction is **6.9x** and **9.9x** the flow; the "10-14x" figure was the two percentages read as multipliers. Across the **36 contracts with a file on 2024-08-05** the ratio runs from **0.56** (ENSUSD_PERP, where the forced flow *exceeds* the net open-interest reduction) to **34.2** (DOTUSD_PERP), median 9.94, with 3 of 36 contracts in which the forced flow exceeds the net reduction; and for one contract across three consecutive days it runs 8.2 (2024-08-04), 6.9 (2024-08-05) and 4.3 (2024-08-06). **Consequence: the open-interest-clearing proxy is a poor stand-in for a liquidation event, there is no fixed conversion constant, and the ratio must be reported per contract and per day** — which is the answer to the question the thirteenth cycle left open, and the answer is negative.

5. **The first cross-sectional measurement of forced unwinds on the record — the handoff's open question, answered.** On 2024-08-05, **36 of the 45 COIN-M perps have a file**; the registered statistic (sell liquidation contracts over opening open interest) runs from **0.26% (TRXUSD_PERP)** to **37.94% (ENSUSD_PERP)**, with the majors in the middle (**BTCUSD_PERP 1.96%, ETHUSD_PERP 2.35%, SOLUSD_PERP 1.99%, BNBUSD_PERP 0.93%**); the SELL share of forced contracts is **99-100% for most contracts** (BTCUSD_PERP 86.4%, SOLUSD_PERP 87.1%, ETHUSD_PERP 92.4%); the open-interest reduction is **negative for every one of the 36 contracts**, from -0.98% (AAVEUSD_PERP) to -40.81% (RUNEUSD_PERP) with most between -10% and -32%, i.e. a market-wide forced-unwind signature; the total forced selling across the 36 contracts is **3,612,966 contracts, $65.2m** at the per-symbol multipliers. On 2024-08-04, 35 of 45 have a file and the statistic runs from 0.045% (BCHUSD_PERP) to 1.31% (GMTUSD_PERP), with BTCUSD_PERP at 0.482%. **Consequence: yes, the record gives a free cross-sectional view of forced unwinds — and its heterogeneity is two orders of magnitude, so the aggregation weighting (equal-weight against notional-weight) must be preregistered, because the two differ by an order of magnitude on the same day.** No return, Sharpe, drawdown or hit rate was computed for any strategy; this is a construction description.

6. **The free depth ladder has twelve percentage levels, not ten.** The ladder is -5%, -4%, -3%, -2%, -1%, **-0.20%**, **+0.20%**, +1%, +2%, +3%, +4%, +5% of the mid, at 2,880 distinct timestamps per day (nominal 30-second cadence, ±1-3 second jitter, 34,560 rows per day). The thirteenth cycle's list omitted the two inner **±0.20%** bands, which is the band a market liquidation order actually executes in, so a slippage estimate built on the recorded specification would have jumped the ladder from 1% to 0.20% and overstated the cost of crossing the inner book. This is a correction to a specification a validator would otherwise have built from.

7. **The archive's own `LastModified` field is a free revision detector, and the futures tree is revised outside the published update log.** A file whose `LastModified` departs from its expected T+1 publication date has been **re-uploaded**. In the liquidation tree, 49 of 17,182 files deviate and **all 49 carry the single date 2026-04-16** — the backfill in finding 2. The published update log is silent about them: the repository's `updates/` directory holds three artefacts, all from 2022, none covering futures paths, and the README's Updates table has two rows. **This settles the eleventh cycle's open question about futures versioning negatively, and it is a new process rule: the versioning status of a free archive can be established per file, without any update log, at the cost of one listing field.**

8. **A second venue's free dated history now exists, and the centralised one does not — a determinate answer in both directions.** Hyperliquid's public info API is keyless, free and deep: `fundingHistory` returns dated settlements back to **2023-05-11** (the venue's launch) and daily `candleSnapshot` returns dated candles back to **2023-05-12**, both verified live with HTTP 200. Bybit's public archive publishes **trades, premium_index and spot_index** and **no funding and no klines** (HTTP 404 on both), its premium-index and spot-index series cover only **2019-10-01 to 2020-03-10** for eleven coins, and its funding REST returns HTTP 403 from this environment; an independent bulk-downloader had to **remove** its funding and klines functionality because "the data is no longer available on Bybit". OKX's public `funding-rate-history` returns at most the most recent **100 settlements** (2026-09-17 to 2026-10-19, about 33 days) and **ignores both `before` and `after`**. **The thirteenth cycle's recorded route — "the cross-venue leg is constructible from Binance's dated funding-rate archive on one side and OKX's public REST on the other" — is superseded in place: OKX's public REST cannot supply a multi-year dated funding history.** Consequence for CRYPTO-EDGE-003: the free second venue is a **USDC-margined on-chain decentralised exchange** — a different collateral and a different venue class from the entry's specified USDT-margined centralised perps, on the pair where the entry's own persistence sub-claim is *most* likely to fail (a CEX-versus-DEX spread reverts in 1.5-3.5 hours) — and one of the entry's mandatory controls, dated venue open interest, is **not** free there (requester-pays asset contexts; current-only REST).

9. **The Hyperliquid "fill-log start date" conflict is resolved as a prefix question, not a contradiction.** The `node_fills_by_block` prefix starts in late July 2025 while "earlier periods are in the legacy `node_fills` and `node_trades` prefixes", and a third-party indexer states that "all data is fully available from **28-07-2025**" after its own backfill of the native API. A "fill-log start date" is therefore a property of *which prefix* is read, and the catalogue's carried conflict between the 2025-03-27 and 2025-05-25 dates is not a disagreement about the venue's history.

10. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004's size-momentum lead is verified, and the confound it anticipated is now corroborated three times over.** The publication is located and dated: **Xu, Zezhou and Wu, Fenglin, "Size-Momentum Puzzle in Cryptocurrencies", SSRN 6628860, published 1 January 2026** (a working-paper preprint, so its evidential standing is unchanged from the lead), giving the smallest size quintile a **-4.9% weekly reversal spread** against the largest quintile's **+1.0% weekly momentum** and roughly **+4.0% over four weeks** for the large caps. Two stronger sources corroborate the same size-dependence of the sign: **Liu, Tsyvinski and Wu (NBER w25882)** find the momentum quintile spread at +2.7% to +4.1% weekly and the size factor (long the smallest, short the largest) earning **+3.4% weekly**, so small coins have systematically lower forward returns; and **Kozlowski, Puleo and Zhou** find crypto return reversals more pronounced in **smaller and less liquid** coins. **Consequence: the free screen and the published screen can select populations with *opposite signs* of the base effect, so the specification now requires the free universe's size composition to be declared, the unconditional momentum spread to be estimated on the same population, and the comparison to be made within a size stratum.** Without those, a free replication's null is uninterpretable in either direction.

11. **The one promotion candidate's promotion condition was tested, and it failed on a ground that is now dated rather than merely absent.** The candidate is the liquidity-withdrawal state as the pre-cascade severity channel. The free depth ladder runs to **2026-10-19**, but the forced-flow record ends **2024-10-14** by the publisher's discontinuation, so the decisive October 2025 cascade remains **unobservable in forced-flow terms on Binance** — the outcome variable and the depth ladder do not overlap. The candidate stays deferred, and the reason is now a dated, citable fact rather than an absence of data.

12. **No hypothesis was added and no score moved: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45**, each re-derived from its seven dimension contributions. The cycle's findings are corrections to the catalogue's own facts and specifications, access determinations, and one verification that turns a recorded *hypothetical* confound into a documented one — and the catalogue's standing rule, that evidence bearing on measurement, cost, data access or a base effect does not move a score, applies unchanged. Three **recorded disagreements** are carried, one per active entry, and are recorded rather than dismissed: falsifiability 4/5 → 5/5 for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 (total 60), free-data accessibility 4/5 → 5/5 for CRYPTO-EDGE-003 (total 55), and free-data accessibility 3/5 → 2/5 for CRYPTO-EDGE-004 (total 43).

Scoring rubric (weighted): causal mechanism 20%, evidence 15%, robustness 15%, free-data access 15%, net value after frictions 15%, falsifiability 10%, originality 10%.

### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added.** The cycle opened *primary venue documents* (the publisher's own issue tracker, which is a dated, citable, staff-answered record of what the publisher provides and when it stopped providing it), *corrections* (four), and *access determinations* (three venues for the second-venue funding leg, one archive's revision status), and every one of those was folded into existing entries' specifications rather than spun into an entry. The one promotion candidate — the liquidity-withdrawal pre-cascade severity channel — had its **promotion condition tested**, and failed on a ground that is now dated.
- **Four corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts, marked superseded in place.** (i) The **contract multiplier is per symbol** ($100 for the BTCUSD contracts, $10 for the eight other symbols checked), so the recorded "notional is `quantity x 100`" is narrowed and the naive-product error is larger for every non-BTC contract. (ii) The **"10-14x" proxy-to-flow ratio** was a mis-reading of the two percentages it rested on; the true ratios are 6.9x and 9.9x, and the ratio is unstable (0.56x-34.2x across contracts). (iii) The **"ten-level" depth ladder** is twelve levels, including the ±0.20% bands where a liquidation order executes. (iv) The **"no liquidation dataset anywhere in the USDⓈ-M tree"** fact is true of the archive today and false of its history: the UM record existed for 2023-06-25 to 2024-03-31 and was withdrawn.
- **One new dataset record, the record's own structure.** The liquidation file set is a **64%-complete panel** with a declared, structured absence (9,652 of 26,834 symbol-days), and the two absence regimes — a symbol-level one coherent with zero-event days and a date-level one that is a publisher-side failure — are now distinguished by evidence, with a **mandatory treatment** attached.
- **Five new process rules** and one new standing constraint, recorded in PROCESS_CHANGELOG.md: *a file count is not an observation count*; *a missingness treatment must be declared, and a cross-sectional denominator must be the live universe and not the files present*; *a contract multiplier must be derived per symbol from two independent venue files and never assumed from a family*; *the archive's `LastModified` is a free revision detector*; *a documented product discontinuation is a data-supply fact and not a data-availability fact, and "not provided now" is not "never existed"*; and the standing constraint that a requester-pays archive is admissible only where no free substitute reaches the same fact, now with a worked case (Hyperliquid's dated free funding and price history versus its requester-pays asset contexts).
- **One external question closed in both directions.** The second venue's free dated history exists (Hyperliquid) and the centralised one does not (Bybit, OKX), which is a determinate negative for the route the thirteenth cycle recorded as available.
- **One lead discharged and its confounder promoted from hypothetical to documented**: the size-momentum source is located and dated, and the size-dependence of the crypto momentum sign is corroborated by three independent sources.

## Previous cycle (thirteenth research cycle)

**Superseded in part by the fourteenth cycle above; retained for the audit trail.** Three statements in this section were **corrected by the fourteenth cycle** and are read with that in mind: the **"10-14x" proxy-to-flow ratio** in finding 4 and in the table's rank-1 row is a mis-reading of the two percentages it rests on (the true ratios are **6.9x** for BTCUSD_PERP and **9.9x** for ETHUSD_PERP, and the ratio is **not** stable — 0.56x to 34.2x across the 36 contracts with a file on 2024-08-05); the **"~30-second ladder at 1-5% bands"** description in finding 5 and in the table's rank-1 row understates the ladder, which has **twelve** percentage levels including **±0.20%**; and the **"a ten-level ladder"** description of the `bookDepth` tree omits the two inner bands. The **17,182 symbol-days** figure is a *file* count: the record is a **64%-complete panel** in which **9,652 of 26,834 symbol-days are absent (36.0%)** and an absent file is not automatically a zero-event day.

A **discovery-and-adversarial cycle**. No hypothesis was added and no score moved; what the cycle produced is **one free dataset that no prior cycle found and that changes what a validator can actually build** (a per-contract, per-event, side-attributed forced-liquidation record on Binance's own COIN-M tree, with three silent-failure construction hazards identified before any interpretation), **one new free dataset that makes a published forward-looking framework computable** (the daily order-book depth ladder, 2023-01-01 to the present, which is the input set SaR requires), **the closing of the two highest-value external questions the twelfth cycle left open** — the funding-extreme split and the cross-venue carry comparison — **both now answered, and neither answer favourable to the entry it bears on**, and **a new adverse finding on CRYPTO-EDGE-004's base effect, now in a peer-reviewed source**.

1. **A free, Binance-native, per-contract, per-event, side-attributed forced-liquidation record exists — on the COIN-M (inverse) tree — and no prior cycle found it.** The path is `data/futures/cm/daily/liquidationSnapshot/{SYMBOL}/{SYMBOL}-liquidationSnapshot-YYYY-MM-DD.zip`, with a `.CHECKSUM` beside every file. Enumerated over the whole tree: **118 symbol directories** (the COIN-M perpetuals BTCUSD_PERP, ETHUSD_PERP, SOLUSD_PERP, ADAUSD_PERP and the quarterly delivery contracts), **17,182 symbol-days**, all inside a publisher window of **2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14**. The schema is `time,side,order_type,time_in_force,original_quantity,price,average_price,order_status,last_fill_quantity,accumulated_fill_quantity`; every observed row is LIMIT / IOC / FILLED; timestamps are epoch milliseconds at 1-30 second spacing, so the record is per-event, not a 5-minute snapshot. Access class 1: free, public, no key.

2. **Three construction hazards inside it, all silent-failure, all found before any interpretation.** (i) **Every event is written twice** — verified on five sampled days, the raw row count was exactly twice the distinct-row count and the multiplicity set was exactly {2}, so a naive notional sum *doubles* the statistic. The same hazard class appears independently in the Hyperliquid fill log, where Garcia Seuma (arXiv:2608.03616) records that "the liquidation object rides both legs of each trade, so all totals are deduplicated by the liquidated-user leg or they double-count": two venues, one hazard. (ii) **The quantity is in contracts, not in the asset**, and the COIN-M contract multiplier is 100 USD per contract (Binance's own specification), so notional is `quantity x 100` and **not** `quantity x price`; on 2024-08-05 the intuitive product gives **$17.06bn** of forced selling for BTCUSD_PERP where the correct figure is **$32.3m**, a **528-fold** overstatement that would have made the tail statistic look enormously powerful. (iii) **`side` is the order side**, so a SELL row is a liquidated **long** and a BUY row a liquidated short; the registered statistic counts SELL rows and must halve them for (i).

3. **The USDⓈ-M gap is now a determination, not an inference, and the catalogue's recorded fact is superseded in place.** Every prefix in the bucket was enumerated: `data/futures/um/daily/` holds aggTrades, bookDepth, bookTicker, indexPriceKlines, klines, markPriceKlines, metrics, premiumIndexKlines and trades and nothing else; `data/futures/cm/daily/` alone adds **liquidationSnapshot**; `data/option/daily/` holds only BVOLIndex and EOHSummary; `data/spot/daily/` only aggTrades, klines and trades. **There is no liquidation dataset anywhere in the USDⓈ-M tree** — and USDⓈ-M is the instrument CRYPTO-EDGE-001 specifies. The recorded fact "there is no liquidation dataset anywhere in the archive" was true of the UM tree and false of the CM tree, and is now narrowed rather than deleted.

4. **The registered tail statistic of CRYPTO-EDGE-001 is now computable on free data, in the venue's own contract unit, and the mandated proxy-fidelity step is now executable — and its first measurement is adverse to the proxy.** The COIN-M `metrics` tree carries the 5-minute `sum_open_interest` in **contracts** and, marker-paged, runs to **2026-10-19**, so it *does* overlap the liquidation window; the statistic "sell liquidation contracts / open interest contracts" is dimensionless and price-invariant by construction. The first same-unit measurement, on 2024-08-05 (a single-day construction description, not a performance statistic): BTCUSD_PERP recorded 322,865 SELL contracts = **1.96% of the opening open interest** against a **-13.6%** open-interest reduction, i.e. the forced flow accounts for **14.4%** of the state change; ETHUSD_PERP recorded 2,147,567 SELL contracts = **2.35%** against **-23.3%**, i.e. **10.1%**. The statistic separates the cascade day from the two adjacent days by roughly 4-5x (0.482% and 0.247% for BTC), and **the open-interest-clearing proxy registers a state change 10-14x larger than the forced flow that accounts for it** — so the proxy is a direction-and-severity indicator, not a measure of the flow, and the ratio must be reported by any validator.

5. **The free order-book depth tree was found, and it makes SaR computable on Binance.** `data/futures/{um,cm}/daily/bookDepth/{SYMBOL}/` runs **2023-01-01 to 2026-10-19** for both families, schema `timestamp,percentage,depth,notional`: a ten-level ladder at -5%..-1% and +1%..+5% of the mid, each level carrying depth and USD notional, at roughly 30-second sampling (2,880 timestamps per day). SaR (arXiv:2603.09164) requires exactly this kind of current-book microstructure and was demonstrated on a venue whose data is not free; the free Binance substitute reaches the core metrics (SaR, ESaR, TSaR) but **not** the framework's maker-concentration adjustment, because a bucketed ladder carries no maker identity. `data/futures/um/daily/bookTicker/` covers 2023-05-16 to 2024-03-30.

6. **The funding-extreme split is still not replicated, and the adjacent evidence is now self-contradictory rather than merely absent.** Three independent 2026 sources outside the Shek programme, none reproducing the conditional design: a journal article on Binance BTCUSDT funding 2019-2026 finds **extreme negative funding followed by +0.506% at 24 hours** (significant, FDR-robust) and no effect at the positive extreme; a practitioner study over 789 days on six assets and three venues finds the **bottom decile predicts nothing on any asset**; and a public survivorship-free repository over 791 perpetuals and 2.43m settlements finds the **90-day z-score predicts nothing at any horizon** and a funding coefficient that **changes sign between a 2020-21 reversal regime and a 2023-26 continuation regime**. CRYPTO-EDGE-002's rejection is therefore **corroborated, not disturbed** — and the reinstatement condition must now specify which of three mutually inconsistent results it is trying to overturn.

7. **The cross-venue funding comparison the twelfth cycle asked for now exists, twice, and it is against the thresholded selection.** In the entry's own family, venue set and window, a threshold rule on the trailing cross-venue spread "earns the most gross income of any rule tested and finishes at **-9% to -15% annualised** after costs", while the rule that barely trades — hold continuously, revisit only *which* venue to short, on a 30-day clock — returns **+2.7% to +5.4% annualised net**; and a preregistered 11-gate public test of the archetype **retired** it at 3 of 11 gates with a profit factor of 0.107, diagnosing "the archetype dies on COST, not on no-edge". A third source with an open replication package finds every "smart" refinement fails to beat the naive baseline, and a fourth (practitioner) finds the single-venue version of the signal loses 10% annualised from 2023 while the cross-venue version does not. The peer-reviewed member of the set adds that only 40% of significant spreads survive costs with forced exits in 95% of opportunities. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003's registered *price* mapping is untested and the falsification-handling rule is not engaged**, but the entry's cost case is now externally corroborated at its floor score, and its marginal value is located in one place only — the sign.

8. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004's base effect is now documented as close to non-existent in a peer-reviewed source, and a public executable replication of the entry's own construction exists and depends on a paid data tier.** Han, Kang and Ryu, *Review of Asset Pricing Studies*, published 13 October 2026 (doi 10.1093/rapstu/raag011, abstract read from the Crossref record after the publisher's page returned HTTP 403): "many cross-sectional momentum portfolios are liquidated, and those with statistically significant mean returns often earn negative profits… evidence of time-series momentum is strong, whereas evidence of cross-sectional momentum is almost nonexistent". Separately, a public strategy listing publishes this entry's construction in executable form — 20-day cumulative log return, cross-sectionally demeaned, unit gross exposure, `exposure = clip(median(dispersion history) / current_dispersion, 0.10, 1.0)` with recursive smoothing lambda = 0.80 and a one-day lag, daily rebalancing, with Bitcoin realized volatility (30d) and average off-diagonal correlation (60d) as the rival state variables — and takes **CoinGecko Pro** for prices, market capitalisations and trading-activity screens, i.e. paid data for precisely the market-cap screen the catalogue records as unobtainable free. A new size-composition lead (Xu and Wu) is recorded as a **lead, not evidence**, because the underlying publication was not verified this cycle.

9. **The select-versus-harvest prior gains a second, cost-side leg and is now corroborated in the catalogue's own family by four independent dated 2026 sources plus one peer-reviewed.** It remains a *prior* rather than a rule, but it is now one of the best-supported general regularities the catalogue holds, and it bites in two places: CRYPTO-EDGE-003's thresholded selection, where the net loss is now documented twice, and CRYPTO-EDGE-004's daily-rebalanced cross-sectional book, where the cost side is now quantified by a peer-reviewed source (portfolios liquidated, negative profits) and by an independent 15-minute study (a gross edge of 1.3bp per trade against a 5bp round trip). The requester-pays question that the twelfth cycle left open is **moot for the Binance-native version**: the free `bookDepth` tree reaches the same fact at zero egress cost, so the rule recorded is that a requester-pays archive is admissible only where no free substitute reaches the same fact.

10. **No hypothesis was added, and the one promotion candidate the new data made measurable was examined and kept deferred, with a preliminary score of 43/100.** The candidate is the **liquidity-withdrawal state as the operative pre-cascade severity channel** — the only mechanism in the published literature that the newly free depth data would make testable. It is kept deferred for three reasons: the primary published source states that *"none of the scalar pre-state measures we can construct grades [severity]"* and that the branching account "is then eliminated as a description of the pre-cascade state" at simulated power >= 0.96, so the candidate's own mechanism source argues against it; the **outcome** variable (the forced unwind) is not measurable on the venue whose free depth ladder exists, so a free test must substitute price impact, halving the claim; and the payoff is a low-frequency tail overlay on an exogenous trigger. The preliminary score on the catalogue's rubric is 43/100 (mechanism 3/5, evidence 1/5, robustness 1/5, free data 4/5, net value 1/5, falsifiability 3/5, originality 2/5), below the active rank-3 entry, and it is recorded in REJECTED_AND_DEFERRED.md so that it is not rediscovered.

11. **The record's own state was checked, and it is not what the handoff implied — and the catalogue's pagination defect bit the researcher in this cycle.** The twelfth research cycle updated the handoff only: the five research artefacts still carried the eleventh cycle's contents, and the twelfth cycle's review survives only in HANDOFF.md. The thirteenth cycle is therefore the first to write artefacts since the eleventh. Separately, the recorded pagination rule was re-applied to a *new* tree and immediately produced a false conclusion: a naive first-page listing of the COIN-M `metrics` tree ends at 2022-11-24 for BTCUSD_PERP and appears to show that the open-interest denominator is unavailable for the liquidation window, whereas marker-paging shows it running to 2026-10-19. The same naive read reproduces the eleventh cycle's recorded truncation date exactly on a second tree (UM 1d klines for BTCUSDT, naive tail 2021-05-13). The rule is re-stated in the process changelog in the form that transfers: **the pagination rule must be applied to every listing, including one read for the first time.**

Scoring rubric (weighted): causal mechanism 20%, evidence 15%, robustness 15%, free-data access 15%, net value after frictions 15%, falsifiability 10%, originality 10%.

### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added**, with the reason recorded in item 10: the cycle opened *data* (a free per-event forced-liquidation record, a free order-book depth ladder, a free bookTicker tree, an exhaustive enumeration of the whole archive's tree structure) and every one of those was folded into existing entries' specifications rather than spun into an entry. The one promotion candidate — the liquidity-withdrawal pre-cascade severity channel — was examined and kept deferred with a preliminary 43/100.
- **One new free dataset record and one new free dataset.** (i) **Binance COIN-M `liquidationSnapshot`**: per-contract, per-event, side-attributed forced liquidations, 118 symbols, 17,182 symbol-days, 2023-06-25 to 2024-10-14, access 1, with three silent-failure construction hazards (exact row duplication, the 100-USD contract multiplier in place of a price product, and the order-side semantics of `side`). (ii) **Binance `bookDepth`**: a ~30-second depth ladder at 1-5% bands with depth and USD notional per level, UM and CM, 2023-01-01 to 2026-10-19, access 1, which makes the published SaR framework computable on free Binance data except for its maker-concentration adjustment.
- **Two recorded facts superseded in place, not deleted.** (i) "There is no liquidation dataset anywhere in the archive" — narrowed to the USDⓈ-M tree, and false for the COIN-M tree. (ii) "The published liquidation-share statistic is not computable on free data" — narrowed to the entry's specified instrument; it is computable on the Binance inverse family over a 472-day window, in a dimensionless contract unit.
- **Two new process rules** and one new standing constraint, recorded in PROCESS_CHANGELOG.md: (i) *a dataset's publication window is not the instrument's life, and the two must be recorded separately*; (ii) *a quantity field's unit must be taken from the venue's contract specification, and the obvious product (quantity x price) is the wrong one for inverse contracts*; and the standing constraint that **a requester-pays archive is admissible only where no free substitute reaches the same fact**.
- **Two new external questions closed.** (i) The funding-extreme conditional split remains unreplicated and its adjacent literature is now three-way inconsistent. (ii) The cross-venue carry comparison against unthresholded patient carry now exists twice and is adverse to the thresholded construction.
- **One new adverse finding on CRYPTO-EDGE-004's base**, now peer-reviewed, and one new size-composition lead recorded as a lead rather than as evidence.

## Previous cycle (eleventh research cycle — the twelfth cycle was review-only and wrote no artefacts; its record is in HANDOFF.md)

A **correction cycle**. No hypothesis was added and no score moved; what the cycle produced is **four corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts** — all of them *silent-failure* defects, which is the dangerous kind — and **two access determinations that change what a validator can actually do**. The tenth cycle's specifications were checked against the constructions they describe, and two of the three active entries' constructions were found to be wrong in ways that would have produced false answers rather than errors.

1. **The catalogue's recorded archive-pagination rule is wrong, and following it would have produced false terminal dates on essentially every symbol.** The recorded access note says the S3 listing endpoint "paginates with `<NextContinuationMarker>` rather than `<NextMarker>`". Inspected live on 2026-10-20, that is not what the endpoint does: with **no** delimiter the response contains **no continuation element at all** (only `<Marker>`, which merely echoes the request) and is truncated; with **a** delimiter it carries a standard `<NextMarker>`. The working method is therefore *no delimiter, and page by supplying `marker=<the last key returned>`* — verified end-to-end. A validator following the recorded rule finds no continuation token, concludes the listing is complete, and silently takes the **first** 1000 keys, whose last entry is the symbol's **500th** daily file. Measured on 20 sampled symbols, **20 of 20 were truncated**, and the naive "terminal date" is a mid-life date (BTCUSDT 2021-05-13, ETHUSDT 2021-05-13, SOLUSDT 2022-01-26, DOGEUSDT 2021-11-21, TONUSDT 2025-07-13, SUIUSDT 2024-09-13, and fourteen more). For CRYPTO-EDGE-004's point-in-time universe this is fatal in the *opposite* direction to the one the entry was reinstated to fix: still-trading contracts would be treated as delisted, at a mid-sample date, corrupting membership and momentum ranking together.

2. **The tenth cycle's terminal-state taxonomy is refuted, its rule is validated, and the correction was itself a correction.** The tenth cycle had amended "a delisted contract's series ends" versus "an inactive contract's series continues". Direct reads on 2026-10-20 show a **delisted** contract's series *continuing* with zero-volume, zero-trade files and a frozen close (IPUSDT, IPUSDC, PROMPTUSDT, 1000000BOBUSDT, RVVUSDT, YALAUSDT — all six continue), while the tenth cycle's LUNAUSDT case was delisted and *ended*. The terminal shape is a property of the venue's file emission and is **unrelated to delisting status**. The **rule** — last trading date = the last date with a non-zero trade count — survives, and is now **validated against an independent reference**: the venue's own dated delisting notices give the exact settlement datetime, and in all six instances checked the archive's last non-zero-trade date equals the announced settlement date exactly.

3. **The venue announcement feed is CONFIRMED: free, dated, machine-readable, and multi-year. This reverses the tenth cycle's "unconfirmed from this environment" determination and closes the feed sub-question of the deferred delisting family.** The CMS article catalog endpoint (`.../cms/article/catalog/list/query?catalogId=161&pageNo=N&pageSize=20`, HTTP 200, no key) plus the article detail endpoint (`.../article/detail/query?articleCode=<code>`, which returns `publishDate` as epoch milliseconds, the catalog name and id, and the body) yield **442 delisting articles across 23 pages back to 2022-02-24, of which 76 are perpetual-contract notices**, each giving the exact settlement datetime and the exact contract symbols. The public HTML detail pages carry the same content with a human-readable publication line, so the tenth cycle's record that the announcement pages sit behind a WAF captcha gate is true only of the *listing* page, not the detail pages or the CMS API. Bybit, Coinbase and BitMEX publish the same class of notice with the same mechanics, and **the venues' own settlement rules are now known**: Binance suspends the Futures Insurance Fund in the final hour and executes any resulting liquidation as a single IOCO offloaded into the market, with any unfilled portion resolved through auto-deleveraging; Bybit settles on the average index price of the **30 minutes** before delisting and Coinbase on the **60 minutes** before. The tenth cycle's own labelling of the failed access check as an *environment limitation* rather than a determination was the right call and is vindicated by the check succeeding later from a different route.

4. **The tenth cycle's binding question for CRYPTO-EDGE-001 is substantially answered, and the answer is partly adverse to the catalogue's own specification.** (i) The free open-interest-clearing proxy is a **state**; the payoff is a **flow**; and Garcia Seuma says so in print — "for the intraday analysis we necessarily observe the consequences of forced liquidation (open interest, aggressor flow) rather than liquidations themselves". The one window where both are published is the October 2025 cascade: open interest cleared 25-70% (to 0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid, -24.6% intraday on Binance BTC) while $0.96bn of book-hitting forced sales crossed eighty 0.25%-wide price buckets, $733m of them after onset. (ii) **The v1.5 proxy had a units defect that would have produced a false positive**: it was specified on `sum_open_interest_value`, the **USD-notional** series, which moves with price, so a price decline with an unchanged contract count registers as a "clearing" containing zero liquidations. The same free metrics file carries `sum_open_interest`, the **contract-count** series, verified on 2026-10-20 (BTCUSDT, 2026-10-19 00:55 UTC: 93855.955 contracts, $7,721,663,336.37 notional). The proxy is now re-specified on the contract-count series. (iii) **The proxy's fidelity is now testable rather than arguable**, because two free forced-liquidation records exist: the **Hyperliquid on-chain forced-fill record** (per contract, per fill, with direction, 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30, used by the AFT 2026 paper) and the **Zenodo daily eleven-venue panel** (365 days, $176.6bn, CC-BY-4.0, downloaded and inspected on 2026-10-20). The tenth cycle's statement that the published "sell liquidations as a share of open interest" statistic is "not computable on free data" is **too strong and is narrowed**: it is not computable from the Binance archive, and it *is* computable on free data on Hyperliquid and at daily cross-venue granularity.

5. **The state-variable question is now answered in a specific way, and the answer is adverse to CRYPTO-EDGE-004's framing without moving its score.** The **dispersion** variable — the entry's variable — has **no independent published test at all**: its only source is the entry's own working paper, plus two killed preregistered dial tests. The two variables that *have* been independently tested on comparable coin-level panels are the market-wide **volatility** regime (JRFM 19(8):599) and, in that same paper's own section 4.5, the **average-pairwise-correlation** state variable, tested directly as the candidate mechanism for the regime-dependence it finds and reported as unsupported. A third, the **market-state transition** (Finance Research Letters 2025), competes as well. And the cross-asset dependence structure is itself **non-stationary** — arXiv:2606.16840 documents a tail-dependence regime high through 2022, troughing mid-2024 after the spot-ETF approval, recovering into the late-2024/2025 rally, with the tail-dominance flip left informally dated. The honest reading is that CRYPTO-EDGE-004's claim is more likely **not the operative variable** than **wrong** — the framing the tenth cycle adopted, now with evidence.

6. **Two new sources bear on the *base* effect that CRYPTO-EDGE-004 conditions, one favourable and one adverse, and the score is still held.** arXiv:2510.14435v4 shows crypto size, momentum and value smart beta robust on a **survivorship-aware** universe of 18,622 live plus 29,230 delisted coins with historical data, which is directly adverse to the catalogue's survivorship-dependence objection. Han, Kang & Ryu (2024) finds the opposite on tradability: on a survivorship-aware sample, cross-sectional momentum evidence is **weak** while time-series momentum is strong, and many momentum portfolios are liquidated once daily fluctuations and costs are accounted for. A reader could argue robustness 1/5 → 2/5 (48/100, four points behind CRYPTO-EDGE-003 rather than seven). **The eleventh cycle does not make that move** and records the counter-argument instead, because the new favourable source bears on the base effect on a *different universe with a different screen and a different asset class*, while the entry's own robustness question is about the registered state-dependence claim — still untested, still with two killed adjacent preregistered tests, and whose base spread has been measured net of costs on tradable perpetuals and found not to be distinguishable from zero.

7. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 gains a mandatory construction amendment from a primary venue document, and a recorded disagreement about the tenth cycle's downgrade placement.** Because a venue settles a delisted contract against the **index average over a disclosed pre-settlement window**, the venue-specific basis at settlement is **mechanically zero**; a cross-venue relative-value book therefore cannot hold a basis position through a settlement at all, and the entry's construction must exclude contracts with an announced settlement inside the window and be flat on the delisting venue from the restriction time. This also **closes** the preregistered tier's "unknown" — the sign of a cross-venue book through a delisting settlement is not an empirical question but a position-hygiene question, and there is no position to have a sign on. On the downgrade placement, the eleventh cycle's conclusion is that the tenth cycle's evidence-quality move (2/5 → 1/5) reached the right number by a slightly wrong route: the three adverse preregistered findings (H66, H70, H72) are about the *form* of the construction (select versus harvest), not about the quality of evidence bearing on the entry's registered mapping, and on that mapping the entry has **never had any supporting evidence at all**. The move is therefore best read as the correction of an earlier mis-score carried through v1.1, v1.2 and the ninth cycle. The disagreement is recorded in the ledger, and the alternative placement (robustness, giving 55/100) is recorded rather than dismissed.

8. **The select-versus-harvest prior gains a cost-side leg, and the tenth cycle's own downgrade is challenged on the point the handoff asked about.** The AFT 2026 conference paper finds that **front-end (retail) traders are 96.0% of three-group liquidation volume** (97.6% excluding October 10, 2025) and that forced liquidations impose a **$344 million** loss on front-end traders against which auto-deleveraging returns **$118 million** — a net transfer away from the retail side. A selection book sits on that side: the names it ranks are the names whose forced unwind arrives, and the market in which it must exit is the one whose liquidity is impaired at the moment of the exit (Lim's two-regime recovery: inside-quote depth ending at 66-79% of pre-event and still contracting). Select-versus-harvest is therefore not only a universe-bias prior; it is a **cost prior**, and it is adverse to both of the catalogue's cross-sectional books. The catalogue's *harvest* books are exposed to the opposite version of the same fact: the forced unwind is where the *losing* side is paid, and a harvest book that holds every paying stream is holding the contracts that get liquidated.

9. **No hypothesis was added, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed.** The eleventh cycle's discoveries opened *data*, not *mechanisms*: a venue announcement feed, a free per-fill forced-liquidation record, a free daily liquidation panel, a free order-book archive. Each is a **measurement** opportunity for an existing entry, and each was folded into the existing entries' specifications rather than being spun into a new one. The one family that could have been promoted — the venue-announced delisting event — was examined and **kept deferred**, on a ground that is now stated in numbers: the feed and the design are in hand, and the family's preliminary score on the catalogue's own rubric is **38/100** (mechanism 3/5, evidence 1/5, robustness 1/5, free-data 4/5, net value 1/5, falsifiability 4/5, originality 3/5), because (i) the announcement is public and dated, so any drift between announcement and settlement is arbitraged on announcement, leaving only the final hour, which is a *conditional* effect (it fires only if a margin breach occurs inside that hour) and therefore leaves a handful of usable events; (ii) the event count is ~76 perpetual notices in 4.5 years, most covering one to three contracts, on contracts that were delisted for weak liquidity; and (iii) the final hour is exactly when spread and depth are worst. The deferral is therefore *upgraded* — its data ground is removed, its identification design is now written, and its remaining ground is net value and event count, which no further research can remove. The search that was run and the reason nothing cleared the bar are recorded in the ledger and in REJECTED_AND_DEFERRED.md.

10. **The archive's update log is not exhaustive, and its coverage of the futures tree is not established — the tenth cycle's "versioned data" rule is narrowed in place.** The README's "Updates" table contains exactly two rows and calls itself "an exhaustive list"; the repository's `updates/` directory contains a **third** published artefact (`2022-10-04_aggregate_trade_updates.csv`) that the table does not list, and the replaced-file enumerations live in the **GitHub repository**, not in the S3 bucket, whose `updates/` prefix is empty. The `.CHECKSUM` mechanism does work — the SHA-256 in `BTCUSDT-1d-2026-10-19.zip.CHECKSUM` was verified byte-for-byte against the downloaded zip on 2026-10-20 — and the single kline event covers **1,659 spot symbols**. So the rule becomes: read the table, read the repository's `updates/` listing directly, apply the enumerated replacements, verify every `.CHECKSUM`, and **record the versioning status of the futures tree as unestablished** instead of assuming the spot rule transfers.

Scoring rubric (weighted): causal mechanism 20%, evidence 15%, robustness 15%, free-data access 15%, net value after frictions 15%, falsifiability 10%, originality 10%.

### New this cycle
- **No hypothesis was added**, with the reason recorded in item 9: the cycle opened *data* (a venue announcement feed, a free per-fill forced-liquidation record, a free daily liquidation panel, a free order-book archive) and every one of those was folded into an existing entry's specification rather than spun into a new entry. The one promotion candidate — the venue-announced delisting event family — was examined and kept deferred, with a preliminary score of 38/100 on the catalogue's own rubric and the remaining ground stated as net value and event count.
- **Two access determinations that change what a validator can do.** (i) The **venue announcement feed is confirmed**: free, dated, machine-readable, 442 delisting articles back to 2022-02-24, 76 of them perpetual-contract notices, with exact settlement datetimes and contract symbols, and with equivalent feeds at Bybit, Coinbase and BitMEX. (ii) **Free liquidation-notional datasets exist outside the Binance archive** — the Hyperliquid on-chain forced-fill record (per contract, per fill, with direction, 2025-03-27 to 2026-06-30) and the Zenodo daily eleven-venue panel (365 days). The tenth cycle's statement that the published liquidation-share statistic is "not computable on free data" is narrowed in place.
- **Four corrections to the catalogue's own recorded facts**, all silent-failure defects and all marked superseded in place rather than deleted: the archive's **listing-pagination rule** (wrong: the endpoint carries no continuation token in no-delimiter mode; 20/20 sampled symbols truncate), the archive's **update-log exhaustiveness and coverage** (the table is not exhaustive and the kline event covers the spot tree only), the **terminal-state taxonomy** (a delisted contract's series continues; the shape is unrelated to delisting status), and the **units of the open-interest-clearing proxy** (it was specified on the price-contaminated USD-notional series; it is now specified on the contract-count series).
- **Two new process rules**, recorded in PROCESS_CHANGELOG.md: (i) *a recorded access fact is a defect until re-verified against the live response shape, because the failure mode of a wrong access note is silent truncation, not an error*; (ii) *a construction must be validated against an independent reference where one exists, and a venue's own dated announcements are such a reference for terminal dates*.
- **One new standing constraint**: a **venue's disclosed settlement mechanic is a mechanical constraint on any book**, not merely context — because a delisted contract is settled against a disclosed index average over a disclosed pre-settlement window, the venue-specific basis at settlement is zero by construction, and any cross-venue book must be flat on the delisting venue by the restriction time.

### Downgraded / changed this cycle
- **No entry was downgraded and no score moved.** All four totals were re-derived from their seven dimension contributions and are unchanged: CRYPTO-EDGE-001 = 58, CRYPTO-EDGE-002 = 46 (rejected), CRYPTO-EDGE-003 = 52, CRYPTO-EDGE-004 = 45. The rank order and the 6-point and 7-point margins are unchanged, and the reason they are unchanged is that the eleventh cycle's findings bear on *measurement and construction*, not on any entry's registered prediction.
- **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 → v1.6**, confidence low. The cycle-10 specification is amended on three points: the proxy is re-specified on the price-invariant contract-count series, a fourth tail statistic is added and is now the preferred arm where the free liquidation records exist, and a **mandatory proxy-fidelity validation step** is added, to be reported before any interpretation. A recorded note that the net-value score (2/5) should arguably be lower now that the post-cascade liquidity impairment is documented.
- **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 → v1.4**, confidence low. One mandatory construction amendment (the delisting-settlement exclusion and forced-flat rule) and a **recorded disagreement** about the placement of the tenth cycle's evidence-quality downgrade, with the alternative placement (robustness, 55/100) recorded rather than dismissed.
- **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 → v1.4**, confidence low. The cycle-10 specification is amended on four points (pagination, terminal state, independent cross-check, narrowed versioning rule), and the tenth cycle's terminal-state description is marked superseded in place. A **recorded counter-argument** on robustness (a favourable new survivorship-aware source that the cycle declines to score, for a stated reason).

## Previous cycle (tenth research cycle)

A **specification cycle**. No hypothesis was added, one entry was downgraded on adverse preregistered evidence, both testable entries now carry complete, preregisterable validation designs, and the ninth cycle's own archive construction was adversarially re-verified - which produced a **correction to its description** and a documented answer to the delisting-announcement feed question that is an environment limitation rather than a data determination.

1. **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 is downgraded 55 -> 52 on evidence quality alone (2/5 -> 1/5)**, confidence low; every other dimension is held. Three independent preregistered results in the same funding family now bear on the entry. H66 documents that **selecting** the top K funding names is monotonically dominated by **harvesting** every name that pays (Sharpe 2.27 / 2.99 / 4.06 for K = 3 / 5 / 10 against 5.60 for harvest-all over the same 34 symbols and window, with drawdown −5.52% against −0.76%); H70 documents the same distinction arriving from the opposite direction - rotation *selects*, carry *harvests*, so a selection rule is the form the evidence treats as weaker - and records the distinction explicitly as "a hypothesis, not a rule"; H72 documents that the harvest form earns approximately zero in 2025-2026 on a point-in-time universe as well as a hindsight one, with its CAGR halving (+4.36% -> +2.35%) even in the years when it was paid, and that the *sign* of the delisting bias on a short-perp book is unknown. None of the three tests the entry's registered mapping, so the entry is not rejected; what changed is that the entry's only content over the patient-carry construction that its own cost arithmetic selects toward is a rule that three independent findings now say not to use, while its own predictive mapping has still never been tested. The cycle-10 specification therefore changes the entry's mandatory bar from a placebo to **prior art in the same family**: the entry must beat unthresholded patient carry on the same clock, window, costs and inference, or its content is nil by construction.

2. **The ninth cycle's archive construction survives adversarial challenge, but its description did not.** The rule - *last trading date = the last date with a non-zero trade count* - is robust, but the ninth cycle's stated mechanism ("Binance keeps publishing a file for a ceased contract with volume=0 ... every later file is zero-volume") is true only of one case. Direct reads on 2026-10-20 show **two distinct terminal shapes**: a *delisted* contract's file series simply **ends** at the delisting date (LUNAUSDT: 13 daily files in May 2022, none after; the final 2022-05-13 file is zero-filled with a frozen close of 0.0080, against 70,710,754,248 volume and 20,104,720 trades on 2022-05-12), while an *inactive but not delisted* contract's series **continues indefinitely** with zero-volume files (OMGUSDT: files still present for 2025-02, 2025-08 and 2026-01, with 2026-01-15 at volume 0, trades 0, close 0.2910, against the last real trade on 2025-01-20). The derived answers are unchanged and were re-verified by direct read; the *description* was wrong for the delisted case, and a validator must record the terminal state per symbol rather than assume one. Two further corrections: the kline tree has an **interval subdirectory** (`.../klines/{SYMBOL}/1d/`) that a construction must include or it fetches nothing, and the ninth cycle's notes recorded only that the `daily/metrics/` tree lacks one.

3. **The archive is versioned data and must be treated as such.** Its README publishes an exhaustive, dated update log of retroactive file replacements, and the single kline event listed (2022-08-08, "Fixed inconsistent data") enumerates **73,234 replaced files across 1,659 spot symbols** with original and replacement SHA-256 checksums, while every file also ships a `.CHECKSUM`. Retroactive correction is real but enumerable and auditable: read the log, apply the listed replacements, verify checksums, record the archive revision date. The event is spot-only, so the futures tree used here was untouched by it, but the rule binds every future run. This is a new standing rule in the catalogue, not a one-off.

4. **The "select versus harvest" distinction is now a structural prior for the whole catalogue, and it is adverse to both of its cross-sectional books.** A book that ranks and selects is structurally more exposed to universe bias than one that harvests every paying stream, because a hindsight list hands a selection its winners while it barely changes a harvest's average - and the one measurement in this catalogue that exists (the momentum base effect retaining 42% of its Sharpe on a point-in-time universe) is exactly of the selection type. The same prior, read from the other side, is why the harvest alternative that CRYPTO-EDGE-003 converges on is the robust form and simultaneously the form that earns approximately nothing today.

5. **Two new peer-reviewed sources sharpen CRYPTO-EDGE-004's competing explanation without moving a score.** The Journal of Risk and Financial Management (19(8):599, 7 August 2026, open access) finds that the reliability of cross-sectional crypto signals is *regime-dependent* on a market-wide volatility regime (out-of-sample AUC swinging from 0.43 to 0.79 across years, informative in calm markets and unreliable in turbulent ones), robust across thresholds, proxies, frequencies, cluster-bootstrap inference and a second exchange - and finds the natural co-movement mechanism **unsupported**; and Finance Research Letters (2025, doi 10.1016/j.frl.2025.108356) finds crypto momentum arises only in UP-UP market-state transitions and is absent in DOWN-DOWN and transitional regimes. Both support the entry's *premise* and both make the choice of state variable contested, so the cycle-10 specification now carries a **rival state variable** (pairwise cross-asset correlation, run through the identical statistic) and treats Bitcoin realized volatility as a documented competing driver rather than a nuisance control. CRYPTO-EDGE-004 stays at 45/100: no new source tests its registered prediction, and two of its dimensions are already at their floors.

6. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 gains independent support for its mechanism's premise and a binding tail-versus-mean separation rule, and stays at 58/100.** Christin, Routledge, Soska and Zetlin-Jones's "The Crypto Carry Trade" (CMU working paper; free PDF; a public summary by the author is in the CFTC docket S7-2026-21) documents that shorting perps against spot earned high realised returns across 18 Binance contracts and that funding is typically positive - the premise half of the mechanism - while stating explicitly that the liquidation mechanism "does not establish that perpetual futures are the sole or primary cause of sharp cryptocurrency price movements". Two preregistered results bear on how a crash-conditionality claim must be measured: a crash state in the perp era is followed by a positive cross-sectional return (alts +0.441% net the day after a >3% BTC down day, 727 held days), and extreme up shocks continue (+3.54% over 7 days). A signal that predicts crash frequency may therefore also predict a positive forward return, so **the tail statistic and the mean statistic must be reported separately and never netted** - now a binding rule in the cycle-10 specification, together with the free open-interest-clearing proxy for the tail statistic.

7. **Two external questions are closed and one is answered with an environment limitation.** (a) *The conditional funding-extreme split* (Shek 2026) is still not replicated by any independent party; the adjacent preregistered tier now contains a documented *continuation* result after extreme up shocks, which is the opposite direction to Shek's reversal headline and shows that the statistical form he uses is estimable in crypto panels with block bootstrap and the cross-section intact. (b) *The venue announcement feed* **could not be confirmed from this research environment**: Binance's public REST API returns HTTP 451 ("service unavailable from a restricted location"), its announcement pages return HTTP 405 behind a WAF human-verification gate (`x-amzn-waf-action: captcha`), and the CMS announcement endpoint returns HTTP 404 at the edge, while the S3 archive remains fully accessible. This is recorded as an environment limitation and **not** as a data-availability determination, so the venue-announced-delisting family stays deferred on its identification ground. (c) The delisting-announcement feed remains the binding blocker for the deferred delisting-event family and for nothing else.

8. **No hypothesis was added, and the reason is recorded rather than assumed.** The tenth cycle's search was directed at specification and adversarial review rather than at coverage, and the one new capability the cycle confirmed - an auditable, versioned, point-in-time perp universe with a documented update log - enables no family that is not already closed on cost, on identification, or on prior art: the delisting-event family is blocked by the announcement feed, the token-supply family by the CoinGecko history limit, and the funding-selection family by three independent preregistered results that say the selection form is the weaker one. Adding an entry in any of those families would be adding a worse version of something already recorded.

Scoring rubric (weighted): causal mechanism 20%, evidence 15%, robustness 15%, free-data access 15%, net value after frictions 15%, falsifiability 10%, originality 10%.

### New this cycle (tenth research cycle)
- **No hypothesis was added**, with the reason recorded above (item 8).
- **Two new standing rules entered the catalogue's process**: (i) *absence of data has two shapes and they must be handled separately* - a series that ends and a series that continues with zero-filled values are different facts, so a construction records the terminal state per symbol rather than the terminal date alone; (ii) *a free archive that publishes an update log is versioned data* - read the log, apply the enumerated replacements, verify per-file checksums and record the archive revision date with any result derived from it.
- **One new structural prior**: *select versus harvest* - a cross-sectional book that ranks and selects is structurally more exposed to universe bias than one that harvests every paying stream, and both the exposure and its robustness must be reported whenever a candidate's base effect is a selection.
- **One new specification requirement**: an entry's incremental content must be tested against the **best prior-art construction in its own family**, not only against a placebo, and reported in the incumbent's own units.

### Downgraded / changed this cycle
- **CRYPTO-EDGE-003 downgraded 55 -> 52**, confidence low. Evidence quality 2/5 -> 1/5 only. Mechanism, robustness, free-data accessibility, net value, falsifiability and originality held; the alternative placements of the new evidence (net value and originality) are recorded in the ledger, and net value was already at its floor of 1/5.
- **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 unchanged at 58**, confidence low. No dimension moves: the new sources bear on the mechanism's premise and on competing explanations, not on the registered prediction.
- **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 unchanged at 45**, confidence low, and its construction description corrected in item 10 of the ledger: the rule survives, the terminal-state narrative did not.

## Earlier cycle (ninth research cycle)

The cycle's dominant finding is a **data determination that reverses the eighth cycle's answer**, and it unblocks the catalogue's only non-derivatives entry. No hypothesis was added, one was reinstated, one was downgraded, two external questions were resolved, and the CoinGecko access trap was closed.

1. **The eighth cycle's "no dated delisting record on free data" is reversed, and it was wrong for a methodological reason.** A dated delisting record for tokens a major venue has delisted **is** constructible on free data - not from a vendor field, but by derivation from the Binance public data archive (`https://data.binance.vision`, access 1, no key). The archive keeps a dated daily kline file for every USDⓈ-M perpetual symbol ever listed (998 distinct symbols, including contracts the venue has since delisted), and the terminal file of a contract that has stopped trading carries **volume = 0 and trade count = 0 with a frozen close**. So: listing date = first file with a non-zero trade count; last trading date = last file with a non-zero trade count. Verified against two independently announced venue delistings - LUNAUSDT's last real trade on 2022-05-12 (volume 70.7bn, 20.1m trades) against the venue's 2022-05-13 delisting, and OMGUSDT trading through 2025-01-20 and zero-volume from 2025-02-10, immediately after the venue's announced 2025-01-31 settlement (first announced 2024-12-06, postponed twice) - and against one re-listing (CVCUSDT is zero-volume through 2024 and trades again on 2026-09-15). Two construction hazards are recorded: the archive's **monthly** tree is incomplete for some symbols (ANTUSDT monthly ends 2023-05 while its daily series continues to 2024-05-28; CVCUSDT monthly ends 2024-09 while its daily series continues to 2026-09) and must not be used for lifetime questions; and a symbol's trading lifetime must be reconstructed as **segments**, not one interval, because re-listings occur. The eighth cycle read the archive as a *price* source and concluded it held no dated delisting record; the record was there, encoded as the *absence of trades*. A parallel spot tree with the same logic (BTCUSDT monthly from 2017-08) makes a spot implementation equally constructible.

2. **CRYPTO-EDGE-004 is reinstated: 45/100, confidence low, rank 3.** Its deferral was on a data ground and that ground has been removed; the entry's registered prediction has still never been tested, so the falsification-handling rule remains unengaged and it is not rejected. Two dimensions move, in opposite directions, and the total is unchanged. Free-data accessibility 2/5 -> 3/5: the essential input - a survivorship-aware point-in-time crypto universe - is now buildable, with exact listing dates and exact last-trading dates for 998 perpetual symbols; but not 4/5 or 5/5, because the published result's *market-capitalisation* screen still has no free history, so a free replication must substitute listing-age and trailing-volume screens and is a **replication, not an exact replication**. Net value after frictions 2/5 -> 1/5: Arefev (2026, SSRN 7404139) measures the base cross-sectional momentum spread on a survivorship-aware, 832-symbol, tradable Binance perpetual universe over 2020-2026 and finds that neither permitted specification yields a net-of-costs spread distinguishable from zero - the gross +0.573% per week is the same order as 0.40pp per week of realistic costs, and the alternative specification flips sign on an arbitrary rebalancing-phase choice. The catalogue therefore **regains an active non-derivatives member**, which was the single largest structural gap recorded at the end of the eighth cycle.

3. **CRYPTO-EDGE-001 is downgraded to 58/100, confidence low** - evidence quality 3/5 -> 2/5 and no other dimension moves. Three independent 2026 sources now bear on the transferability of its dated-futures support without testing its registered claim. Garcia Seuma (arXiv:2608.03616, 5 August 2026) measures the October 2025 record cascade in flight and finds it deeply subcritical (lambda-hat approximately 0.1), the transition abrupt and first-order rather than critical, with the in-cascade signature in the liquidity sector - price-impact spikes of 1.2-3.5x regressed and 3.2-9.1x quoted, while open interest clears by 25-70% (-24.6% for Binance BTC, to 0.30-0.55 of baseline on Hyperliquid) - and shows the branching model failing as an account of the *pre-cascade state* on both of its falsifiable predictions at simulated power of at least 0.9; its companion (arXiv:2607.27070) finds the price signature present in five of seven cascades but silent in exactly the two sudden-news shocks, and inverting between the two largest events. Kitron and Wengrowicz (arXiv:2608.21888) find funding, basis, funding-clock, volatility and session conditioning **flat in every case** across 183 Binance pairs. Memon, Anklesaria-Dalal and Mishra (IJFMR 4(4):86388) find that direct comparisons of conditional means between up-shock and down-shock events generally **fail** to establish statistically significant directional asymmetry - which is the statistical form a conditional crash or reversal claim takes. The mechanism score is deliberately held: nothing new contradicts the market-structure description itself.

4. **The third non-derivatives family was found, and it is rejected on cost, not on data.** Short-horizon flow-conditioned reversal (Kitron and Wengrowicz 2026) has free, point-in-time inputs - confirmed present in the same archive (daily `bookDepth/` from 2023-01-01, `aggTrades/`, `trades/`) - and the strongest published result located this cycle: 90% of 183 Binance pairs carry significant 15-minute directional reversal under one matched, strictly out-of-sample walk-forward protocol against 2.7% of 187 US stocks and ETFs, in every focal coin-year since 2021, with a class-mean AUC gap of +0.031 as designed and +0.011 (95% CI [+0.008, +0.014]) under the most conservative accounting, surviving an artifact battery, an exact permutation null and a frozen six-month holdout, with the mechanism documented as compensated liquidity provision after aggressive taker flow. It fails because the **gross edge peaks near 1.3 bp per trade against a 5 bp round-trip cost** - "large enough to detect, too small to clear benchmark spot capture costs". This is the second instance in this catalogue of a robustly detected signal dying only on costs, and it is the cleanest external demonstration that detection robustness and tradability are separate axes in this asset class.

5. **Two external questions are closed.** (a) *CoinGecko*: the 365-day historical limit is a **paid-tier artefact** - CoinGecko's own API reference states that the Demo tier (free, keyless or with a free key) is restricted to the past 365 days, the paid Basic tier to two years, and only Analyst and above carry the full range - so no free key lifts it. CoinPaprika applies the same 365-day limit (verified by direct fetch: "Getting daily historical data before 2025-10-20 ... is not allowed in this plan"), and its `last_data_at` field is the aggregator's last observation, not a cessation date. (b) *CRYPTO-EDGE-001's mandatory open-interest baseline* is now **access 1**, not access 2: the archive's `daily/metrics/` gives 5-minute open interest per symbol continuously from 2020-09-01 to the present (366 daily files for BTCUSDT in 2024, 365 in 2025, 292 in 2026 through 19 September), covering the whole post-July-2024 window. Historical liquidation notionals are **not** archived, so P1's "sell liquidations as a share of open interest" is not directly computable - but the open-interest *clearing* is the documented observable footprint of forced unwinding, and it is now computable at 5-minute resolution.

6. **The conditional funding-extreme split is still not replicated, and its prior is now lower.** No independent replication or refutation of Shek (2026, SSRN 7520320) was located. Two new adjacent 2026 results lean against it: the Memon et al. finding above, which is the closest published setting to Shek's headline statistic, and the flat funding-and-basis conditioning of Kitron and Wengrowicz. Single-source risk is therefore **not** discharged and the deferral stands, with the single-source risk elevated rather than reduced.

7. **Standing prior, extended.** The crash process in crypto is documented to be first-order and shock-driven in its two largest recent instances, with the observable footprint in open-interest clearing and price impact rather than in any pre-crash state variable. Any new candidate in the carry and crowding family must now answer two questions that were not on the list last cycle: *what in the state variable is not already a proxy for the common factor*, and *does the candidate survive on the observable footprint rather than on the precursor*. And no candidate in this family may be promoted on dated-futures evidence alone.
